# The assigned tree-Gibbs trajectory: conditional clocks and entropy payments

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, one
allocated round after completed48/attempted49, with completed ceiling49
and attempted ceiling50. TASK.md and master_guidance d70a595e12f4b50f
authorize this analytical investigation and explicitly forbid computation
and fact_submit. No new fact is claimed or submitted. All new arguments
are reproduced together here; no unverified finding is a premise.

## 1. Verdict and the precise remaining question

The specified terminal inequality is still UNRESOLVED. This draft proves
neither that the specified trajectory reaches the benchmark nor an
asymptotic upper obstruction for that trajectory. Its new interfaces are:

* an exact decomposition of the objective over conditional tree fields;
* an exact formula for every cumulative subtree clock, including its
  dependence on the actual endpoint ancestral coordinates;
* exact conditional relative-entropy and Euclidean-radius payment
  identities, with the squared-step and inter-iterate entropy terms;
* quantitative control of every instantaneous subtree mass by the actual
  entropy, with no assumed concentration;
* an explicit demonstration that the integrated linear occupation
  constraints alone admit endpoint zero on the ORIGINAL word;
* a finite original-benchmark comparison showing that preservation of
  the initial deepest field cannot by itself certify the target. That
  particular certificate needs amplification and an actual overstep in
  its changing conditional clock.

The last two items exclude only stated proof shortcuts. They do not
exclude this tree-Gibbs candidate. Even a positive answer for this
candidate would leave the original all-word/all-horizon bridge open.

## 2. Definitions, legality, and normalization reproduced from scratch

Fix an integer m>=8. Write

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
 n=2^m-1, N=2^m, h=H_m,
 r=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) r^j, w_j=r^j/Zgeom.

Every occurrence of a child word retains these SAME enclosing-m
parameters. Let S_t=sum_(i<t)h_i and S=S_n. Counting the two copies
at each join, or solving S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a*c^j, gives

 1+S=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)
     =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta*c^m,  zeta=beta/[c(c-2)].       (2.1)

Here 0<zeta<1: lambda(lambda-2)=1, c>lambda, and beta<1.

Use one orthonormal coordinate e_v for each binary word v of length
less than m. For a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m), define

 a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))e_(s_1...s_j),
 F(x)=log[N^(-1)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)].                 (2.2)

The feature mean is zero, and every feature has squared norm one.
Different coordinate rows of the feature matrix are orthogonal. Rows
with disjoint supports have zero product. If one node is below the
other, the descendant row has equal positive and negative entries on
its support, on which the ancestor row is constant. Each row has
positive squared norm. Thus the matrix has row rank N-1.

Jensen's inequality applied to the exponential gives F>=0. Equality
requires all leaf logits to be equal; their sum is zero, so they must
all vanish. Full row rank then gives x=0. Consequently F has the UNIQUE
attained minimum zero at zero. The same rank argument shows that the
map from x to the N logits is onto their zero-sum subspace.

Differentiating this finite sum gives grad F=E_p a and Hess F=Cov_p(a),
where p_s=exp(<a_s,x>)/sum_t exp(<a_t,x>). To bound the Hessian, fix a
unit vector u and let A and B be the smallest and largest values of
<a_s,u>. For any random variable z in [A,B],

 E z^2 <=(A+B)E z-AB,
 Var(z)<=(B-A)^2/4-(E z-(A+B)/2)^2<=(B-A)^2/4.

The first inequality follows by averaging (z-A)(B-z)>=0.
If leaves first separate at level j, their squared distance is

             4w_j+2sum_(l>j)w_l.                   (2.3)

The quarter-distances strictly decrease with j, since the difference
between consecutive ones is w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. The diameter bound above
therefore gives the globally valid constant

                  Ltree=(1+w_0)/2.                (2.4)

It is sharp. Choose a pair separating at the root. Prescribe equal
logits for that pair and logits smaller by T for every other leaf,
then subtract the common mean. Surjectivity of the logit map realizes
these logits at an actual finite x. As T tends to infinity the Gibbs
law tends to the equal law on that pair. Its covariance in the unit
feature-difference direction tends to the squared diameter divided by
four, namely Ltree. This establishes the global supremum.

Take t0=log(2) and the exact initial point

 x_0,(0^j)=t0/sqrt(w_j), 0<=j<m,
 x_0,v=0 on every other node.

The squared distance to the whole minimizer set is exactly

 R^2=t0^2 sum_(j=0)^(m-1)1/w_j
    =t0^2 r^(1-m) Zgeom^2.                          (2.5)

The last equality reverses the finite geometric sum. Run the actual
iteration x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Ltree)grad F(x_i). For an arbitrary desired
smoothness L, the function (L/Ltree)F uses exactly steps h_i/L and has
the legal instance score 2F(x_n)/(Ltree R^2). All positions, values,
gradients, and the minimum are from this one finite static function.

## 3. Actual group probabilities and conditional fields

All coordinates off the all-left spine remain zero. At a point on
that spine, leaves with the same first right turn have equal logits.
A non-spine coordinate splits one such group into equal halves, so
its mean feature coordinate vanishes. Induction through every physical
step proves invariance.

Define y_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j)x_(i,0^j). For first exit k<m and the final
all-left leaf k=m, respectively, set

 q_k=2^(-k-1) (k<m), q_m=2^(-m),
 ell_(i,k)=sum_(j<k)y_(i,j)-y_(i,k) (k<m),
 ell_(i,m)=sum_(j<m)y_(i,j),
 P_(i,k)=q_k exp(ell_(i,k))/sum_(u=0)^m q_u exp(ell_(i,u)).  (3.1)

These are probabilities of the original leaves, aggregated only over
identical logits. In particular they are not free control variables.
Let

 Q_(i,j)=sum_(k=j)^m P_(i,k), 0<=j<=m,
 d_(i,j)=Q_(i,j+1)-P_(i,j)=2Q_(i,j+1)-Q_(i,j).

Then Q_(i,0)=1 and the exact coordinate update is

 y_(i+1,j)=y_(i,j)-(h_i w_j/Ltree)d_(i,j).           (3.2)

Initially the denominator in (3.1) is (m+4)/4, each side-group
unnormalized mass is 1/4, and the final unnormalized mass is 1. Thus

 P_(0,k)=1/(m+4) (k<m), P_(0,m)=4/(m+4),
 Q_(0,j)=(m-j+4)/(m+4),
 F(x_0)=log[(m+4)/4].                               (3.3)

For each time define conditional log-partitions recursively by

 A_(i,m)=0,
 A_(i,j)=log[(exp(-y_(i,j))+exp(y_(i,j)+A_(i,j+1)))/2],
 T_(i,j)=y_(i,j)+A_(i,j+1)/2.                       (3.4)

This is the conditional partition on the actual all-left depth-j
subtree with its original uniform conditional reference. The side
subtree contributes one because all its coordinates are zero. Direct
factorization gives

 A_(i,j)=A_(i,j+1)/2+log cosh T_(i,j),
 Q_(i,j+1)/Q_(i,j)=(1+tanh T_(i,j))/2,
 d_(i,j)=Q_(i,j)tanh T_(i,j),
 D_i:=F(x_i)=A_(i,0)=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(-j)log cosh T_(i,j). (3.5)

Every A_(i,j) is nonnegative. In particular the objective is the FULL
sum in (3.5), rather than just its final two-leaf contribution.
The exact conditional-field update is

 T_(i+1,j)=T_(i,j)-alpha_(i,j)tanh T_(i,j)
                   +(A_(i+1,j+1)-A_(i,j+1))/2,
 alpha_(i,j)=h_i w_j Q_(i,j)/Ltree.                 (3.6)

Equation (3.6) retains the changing child partition. Dropping its last
term, or replacing alpha by a fresh copy of the original child word,
changes the actual process.

Since sum_k q_k ell_(i,k)=0, substitution in (3.1) also proves

 D_i=sum_k q_k log(q_k/P_(i,k)).                    (3.7)

The reference law is the FIRST argument of this relative entropy.

## 4. Exact cumulative subtree clocks

For any physical prefix length t and depth 0<=j<m, put

 Cclock_(t,j)=sum_(i<t)alpha_(i,j)
            =(w_j/Ltree)sum_(i<t)h_i Q_(i,j).

The complete history sum admits the exact identity

 Cclock_(t,j)=S_t/(Ltree Zgeom c^j)
               +sum_(l<j)c^(l-j)(t0-y_(t,l)).       (4.1)

To prove it, the relation d_(i,j)=2Q_(i,j+1)-Q_(i,j) and Q_(i,0)=1
give by finite induction

 Q_(i,j)=2^(-j)[1+sum_(l<j)2^l d_(i,l)].

Multiply by h_i and sum. Equation (3.2) gives

 sum_(i<t)h_i d_(i,l)=Ltree(t0-y_(t,l))/w_l.

Finally 2^l/w_l=Zgeom c^l and w_j 2^(-j)=1/(Zgeom c^j), proving (4.1).
There is also the mass version, valid through j=m:

 sum_(i<t)h_i Q_(i,j)
  =2^(-j)[S_t+Ltree Zgeom sum_(l<j)c^l(t0-y_(t,l))]. (4.2)

These formulas hold for every prefix, not just recursive block ends.
They do not assert that a nonlinear trajectory depends only on the
sum of its effective steps.

## 5. Actual entropy and radius payment identities

Let p_i^[j] be the conditional Gibbs law on the leaves of the depth-j
all-left subtree, and let u^[j] be the uniform conditional law there.
For probability vectors p and v with positive entries, define
KL(p||v)=sum_s p_s log(p_s/v_s). Set

 I_(i,j)=KL(p_i^[j]||u^[j]),
 Kstep_(i,j)=KL(p_i^[j]||p_(i+1)^[j]),
 E_(i,j)=sum_(l=j)^(m-1) w_l Q_(i,l)^2 tanh^2 T_(i,l),
 Rsub_(i,j)^2=sum_(l=j)^(m-1)y_(i,l)^2/w_l.

Every symbol uses the SAME actual iterate. Conditioned at depth j,
the subtree gradient has squared norm E_(i,j)/Q_(i,j)^2. Its actual
coordinate update has multiplier h_i Q_(i,j)/Ltree, because global
gradients in that subtree equal Q_(i,j) times conditional gradients.
It follows exactly that

 A_(i+1,j)-A_(i,j)
      =-h_i E_(i,j)/(Ltree Q_(i,j))+Kstep_(i,j),     (5.1)

 Rsub_(i+1,j)^2-Rsub_(i,j)^2
      =-2h_i Q_(i,j)(A_(i,j)+I_(i,j))/Ltree
                         +h_i^2 E_(i,j)/Ltree^2.   (5.2)

For completeness, the finite exponential-family calculation behind
(5.1) is as follows. If A is a conditional log-partition, g=grad A,
and z'=z-vg, then

 KL(p_z||p_z')=<g,z-z'>+A(z')-A(z)
              =v||g||^2+A(z')-A(z).

Here v=h_i Q_(i,j)/Ltree. Also the centered conditional feature mean
at the origin is zero, and

 <z,grad A(z)>=A(z)+KL(p_z||u^[j]).

Expanding ||z-vg||^2 proves (5.2). No limiting, continuous-time, or
independent-child approximation was taken. All probabilities are
strictly positive, so these expressions are finite. Nonnegativity of
each KL follows from -sum_s p_s log(v_s/p_s)>=sum_s(p_s-v_s)=0,
using log z<=z-1.

At the root, these give the exact telescopes

 D_n=D_0-(1/Ltree)sum_(i<n)h_i E_(i,0)
                                  +sum_(i<n)Kstep_(i,0),           (5.3)
 Rsub_(n,0)^2=R^2-(2/Ltree)sum_(i<n)h_i(D_i+I_(i,0))
                                  +(1/Ltree^2)sum_(i<n)h_i^2 E_(i,0). (5.4)

The positive Kstep terms in (5.1) and the squared-step terms in (5.2)
cannot be discarded to claim monotonicity or a terminal upper bound.
The forward conditional entropy has the additional exact decomposition

 I_(i,j)=sum_(l=j)^(m-1)[Q_(i,l)/Q_(i,j)]
          [T_(i,l)tanh T_(i,l)-log cosh T_(i,l)].    (5.5)

Indeed the conditional probability of reaching each spine node is
Q_(i,l)/Q_(i,j), and the divergence of its Bernoulli split from the
equal split is T tanh T-log cosh T. Side subtrees have zero conditional
divergence. This also records explicitly the different weights in
forward entropy (5.5) and reverse entropy (3.5). The same expansion
between consecutive conditional laws gives the further exact formula

 Kstep_(i,j)=sum_(l=j)^(m-1)[Q_(i,l)/Q_(i,j)]
   [log cosh T_(i+1,l)-log cosh T_(i,l)
      -(T_(i+1,l)-T_(i,l))tanh T_(i,l)].             (5.6)

For a single split its log probability is sigma*T-log(2 cosh T),
where sigma=+1 or -1. Taking the old-law expectation of the difference
between the old and new log probabilities gives the bracket in (5.6).
Multiplying by each node's old conditional reach probability and
summing proves the formula. Thus the payments in (5.1) have explicit
values in the same consecutive conditional fields; they are not free
nonnegative slack variables.

## 6. A quantitative mass bound without a concentration assumption

For every time i and depth 1<=j<=m, put u=2^j Q_(i,j) and
Phi(u)=u-1-log u. Then

                  Phi(2^j Q_(i,j))<=2^j D_i.        (6.1)

Split the sum in (3.7) between the subtree and its complement. The
conditional divergences are nonnegative, so D_i is at least the
binary divergence with reference mass p=2^(-j) and current mass Q:

 -p log(Q/p)-(1-p)log[(1-Q)/(1-p)].

Set u=Q/p. The inequality -log(1-z)>=z for z<1, applied to
z=p(u-1)/(1-p), bounds the second term below by p(u-1).
This proves (6.1), including Q<p. Further,

 Phi(u)-(sqrt(u)-1)^2=2[sqrt(u)-1-log sqrt(u)]>=0.

Consequently

 (1-sqrt(2^j D_i))_+^2 <=2^j Q_(i,j)
                         <=(1+sqrt(2^j D_i))^2,     (6.2)
 alpha_(i,j)<=h_i/(Ltree Zgeom c^j)
                         *(1+sqrt(2^j D_i))^2.     (6.3)

These are bounds from the actual entropy at the SAME time; terminal
small entropy alone supplies no bound for earlier clocks. The initial
state (3.3), for example, is very far from uniform at deep levels.

There is also a quantitative endpoint enclosure for (4.1). Define
U_l(D)=arcosh(exp(2^l D)), with arcosh z=log(z+sqrt(z^2-1)) for z>=1.
The nonnegative summands in (3.5) give

 |T_(t,l)|<=U_l(D_t),  0<=A_(t,l+1)/2<=2^l D_t,
 -U_l(D_t)-2^l D_t<=y_(t,l)<=U_l(D_t).

Inserting these into the exact clock formula yields

 Cbase_(t,j)-sum_(l<j)c^(l-j)U_l(D_t)
 <= Cclock_(t,j)
 <= Cbase_(t,j)+sum_(l<j)c^(l-j)[U_l(D_t)+2^l D_t], (6.4)

where Cbase_(t,j)=S_t/(Ltree Zgeom c^j)+t0 sum_(l<j)c^(l-j).
Unlike (6.3), this bounds an entire accumulated clock using the
CURRENT entropy and the elapsed physical step sum. It still does not
control the individual factors in the nonlinear update (3.6).

## 7. Why cumulative occupation constraints do not settle the target

For the ORIGINAL S in (2.1), and every m>=8, terminal y_(n,j)=0 is
compatible with ALL linear integrated gradient equations and positive
time-averaged group occupations. This is an endpoint relaxation only:
the following probability vector is not claimed to be an actual GD
history.

Set K0=Ltree Zgeom t0 and define

 Tbar_j=2^(-j)[S+K0 sum_(l<j)c^l], 0<=j<=m,
 Vbar_j=Tbar_j-Tbar_(j+1)
       =2^(-j-1)[S-K0(c^j-sum_(l<j)c^l)], 0<=j<m,
 Vbar_m=Tbar_m.                                    (7.1)

Then sum_j Vbar_j=S and 2Tbar_(j+1)-Tbar_j=K0(c/2)^j
=Ltree t0/w_j. Thus Pbar_j=Vbar_j/S, if positive, satisfies exactly
the integrated version of (3.2) with terminal zero. We now prove
positivity using the original word rather than an asymptotic schedule.

For m>=8, beta>=7/8 and c<11/4. The latter follows from
sqrt(2)<13/9 and c<=(9/8)lambda. Equation (2.1) gives

 S>=beta*c^(m-1)/(c-2)-1
   >(7/6)c^(m-1)-1>c^(m-1).                        (7.2)

For the final inequality, c^(m-1)>2^(m-1)>=128. Since
Zgeom<c/(c-2), Ltree Zgeom=(Zgeom+1)/2<(c-1)/(c-2).
Also c>2 makes c^j-sum_(l<j)c^l increase with j. Hence its largest
price in (7.1) satisfies

 K0[c^(m-1)-sum_(l<m-1)c^l]
 <t0[c^(m-1)+1/(c-2)]
 <(3/4)[c^(m-1)+5/2]<c^(m-1)<S.                  (7.3)

Here log2<3/4 follows by the strict trapezoid bound for the integral
of 1/t on [1,2], and 1/(c-2)<lambda<5/2. Thus all Vbar_j are
strictly positive, including Vbar_m. This proves the stated relaxation
claim. It does NOT construct an actual trajectory ending at the
minimum, and it does not satisfy or replace the nonlinear identities
(3.1), (3.6), or (5.1). It precisely shows what extra information a
proof using (4.1) must retain: chronological conditional response.

A separate exact counterexample rules out replacing that response by
the sum of clocks. On the root-only slice of the SAME F, all descendants
are zero and F=log cosh y. Its root update is y'=y-k tanh y, where
k=h w_0/Ltree. Start this test state at y=t0, set A=5t0/3, B=A/2,
and use physical steps A Ltree/w_0 and B Ltree/w_0. For order (A,B),
the first step reaches zero because tanh t0=3/5, and the terminal is
zero. For order (B,A), the first state is t0/2 and tanh(t0/2)=1/3,
so the terminal is t0/2-5t0/9=-t0/18. Both orders have the same total
clock A+B. These are actual states and gradients of F, but their test
start and test word differ from the assignment. This refutes the
clock-sum substitution, not the assigned terminal inequality.

## 8. The exact original threshold and a finite deepest-pair screen

Retain the original recurrences

 C_0=B_0=1,
 C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)(C_k+C_l+sqrt(C_k^2+6C_k C_l+C_l^2))/2,
 B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)[2C_k+B_l/2+sqrt(B_l^2+8C_k B_l)/2].

The exact requested bound is D_n>=eta_m, where

 eta_m=Ltree t0^2 r^(1-m)Zgeom^2/(2B_n),
 Theta_m=N eta_m=Ltree t0^2 r Zgeom^2 c^m/(2B_n).    (8.1)

The accepted fact04219554e41d9750 supplies, with Z_1 its denominator
ray constant rather than our geometric sum,

 B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^m Z_1,
 Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000.              (8.2)

The identity B_1=4 follows immediately from its single defining split.
Only this existing exact enclosure is used; no benchmark evaluation,
DP, replay, or unproved numerical constant occurs here.

For every m>=32, there is the explicit finite bound

                    Theta_m>51200/41209>1.         (8.3)

Indeed sqrt(2)<47/33 implies c<=(33/32)lambda<5/2, so r>4/5.
The first sixteen terms of Zgeom give

 Zgeom>5[1-(4/5)^16]>24/5,

since 4^8=65536<78125=5^7. The integral of 1/t on [1,2] is strictly
larger than its midpoint lower bound 2/3, giving t0^2>4/9. Also
Ltree>1/2. The first four terms of the binomial expansion give, for
m>=8,

 (1+1/m)^m >=2+(m-1)/(2m)+(m-1)(m-2)/(6m^2)
            >=2+7/16+7/64=163/64>5/2.

Substitute these bounds and (8.2) into (8.1):

 Theta_m > [(1/2)(4/9)(4/5)(24/5)^2(5/2)/2]
                    *(10000/41209)=51200/41209.

The exact entropy decomposition gives the familiar deepest-pair lower
certificate D_n>=(2/N)log cosh y_(n,m-1). Its being strong enough for
the target would require

             2 log cosh y_(n,m-1)>=Theta_m.         (8.4)

But preservation of the initial amplitude would give only

 2 log cosh y_(n,m-1)<=2 log cosh(log2)=2 log(5/4)<1/2.

This is below (8.3). More strongly, (8.4) requires
|y_(n,m-1)|>1, because log cosh z<=z^2/2, proved by integrating
tanh z<=z for z>=0 and using evenness. Thus a deepest-field-only
certificate needs amplification beyond the initial log2, not merely
persistence of a nonzero initial tail coordinate. The full entropy
could still reach the target through other levels.

At the last level (3.6) has no forcing term:

 y_(i+1,m-1)=y_(i,m-1)-alpha_(i,m-1)tanh y_(i,m-1). (8.5)

If 0<=alpha<=2, then |y-alpha tanh y|<=|y|: for y>=0 use
0<=alpha tanh y<=2y, and then use oddness. Consequently success of
the particular certificate (8.4), for m>=32, requires at least one
actual alpha_(i,m-1)>2. Here the largest original step is
h_max=1+beta*c^(m-2), and h_i/(Ltree Zgeom c^(m-1))<1:
use h_max<2c^(m-2), c>2, and Ltree Zgeom>1. By (6.3) such an index
must satisfy

 N D_i >2( sqrt(2 Ltree Zgeom c^(m-1)/h_i)-1 )^2.   (8.6)

This necessary intermediate-entropy condition uses that SAME actual
h_i. It does not assert that such an index occurs. In particular it
cannot be inferred from an assumed fresh isolated child law.

For orientation only, (8.1), the accepted ray limit, and the elementary
limit (1+1/m)^m->exp(1) give

 Theta_m -> exp(1) L_inf t0^2 r_inf /
                        [2 Z_1(1-r_inf)^2],
 r_inf=2/lambda, L_inf=(2-r_inf)/2.                 (8.7)

Neither this limit nor (8.3) replaces the exact finite threshold in
the remaining problem.

## 9. Two direct routes and what remains missing

Route A attempted to integrate the changing subtree clocks and use
the endpoint constraints to force residual entropy. Equations (4.1)
and (4.2) succeed exactly. The positive occupations (7.1)--(7.3) show
that their linear endpoint relaxation permits D_n=0. This is a scoped
failure of that relaxation, not evidence that the actual endpoint is
zero. The scalar test in Section7 separately defeats replacing
chronological nonlinear updates by their total effective time.

Route B attempted to obtain the threshold from survival of the deepest
initial field. The conditional decomposition is exact, and (6.1)
supplies a quantitative change-of-measure bound. The finite price in
Section8 shows that persistence alone cannot make the deepest-pair
certificate sufficient. Its amplification alternative needs an actual
conditional overstep and the intermediate entropy required by (8.6).
Neither amplification nor its absence has been established on H_m.
This route does not control the contribution of the other levels.

The remaining inequality for the completely specified trajectory is

 sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(m-j)log cosh T_(n,j)
             >= Ltree t0^2 r Zgeom^2 c^m/(2B_(2^m-1))              (9.1)

for every sufficiently large m, or a strict eventual reverse inequality
for this same object. T, A, and Q in (9.1) must be generated by
(3.1)--(3.6), with initial values (3.3) and the original physical word.
A next estimate must control the chronological conditional partition
changes or the inter-iterate conditional KL payments across the large
steps and their retained suffixes. A bound on final mass alone, a
uniform-reset clock, or a deepest-coordinate persistence statement
does not fill this gap. No global or trajectory-specific entropy
monotonicity has been assumed.

## 10. Retrieval and scope record

The verified graph was searched before reasoning; no accepted Gibbs
terminal-entropy or subtree-clock fact was returned. Accepted
04219554e41d9750 is used only for the exact ray enclosure in (8.2).
The preliminaries suggested by shared7ec66d4b933755c3 and the fuller
draft38da1067bcd1a0c8 were rederived in Sections2--3. No discrepancy in
those preliminary formulas was found.

Latest guidance c3dd5f8ea6c8852a was read before the handoff. It retains
this worker's one analytical round and no computation/formal allowance.
Its separate max3 trajectory ledger is not borrowed here. A targeted
shared-memory recall returned no new trajectory result used by this
draft; the proof remains wholly analytical.

Matlas returned Asadi and Abbe, "Maximum Multiscale Entropy and Neural
Network Regularization," arXiv:2006.14614, Lemma2, and a Fisher
information statement from arXiv:1210.2592. The first paper's exact PDF
was downloaded to downloads/asadi_abbe_multiscale_entropy_2006.14614.pdf.
Its AppendixA.1 Lemma2 proof and Lemmas6--7 were read through the web
PDF text. They motivate finite-sum entropy decomposition only. All
identities used here are derived above and do not import an external
theorem as a fact. Source: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2006.14614 .

The arXiv HTML request returned "Cache miss" and a Boyd textbook PDF
web request returned "Timeout fetching". The exact arXiv PDF request
and local download succeeded. No successful local PDF text extraction
is claimed, and no package was installed. An initial shared-memory
response was truncated; the needed exact records were subsequently
read from their permitted shared stores with bounded output. The
preliminary7ec66d4b933755c3 is in the direction store, not conclusion.
A case-sensitive heading lookup in the accepted fact yielded no text;
its actual lower-case proof heading and relevant ray proof were then
read directly. One final draft-edit patch failed to match its context
and made no change; after reading the exact lines it was applied with

No numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra, trajectory replay,

No truth-store files, configuration, runtime counters, other worker

# C97: a chronological Markov construction with positive affine values

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Actual runtime round 1 of the
current two-round wave; baseline 27, completed ceiling 29, attempted ceiling
30 including the historical interruption. No numerical evaluation, solver,
machine algebra, replay, subordinate agent, or formal verifier is used.
The original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile Goal remains OPEN.

The only accepted mathematical predecessors used are
`1252b8823bb5a290`, for the original all-directed-pair interpolation
convention and its explicit static conjugate, and `b98bb807d514e80d`,
for the original benchmark convention and its crude exact bound.
All new results below are proved jointly. No C90--C96 assertion is a
mathematical predecessor. Probability is a coordinate construction,
not a replacement for any interpolation inequality.

## 1. Definitions and the three scoped conclusions

Work with smoothness 1 on a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space.
For a supplied finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), actual GD means

    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i,  g_i=grad F(X_i),
    f_i=F(X_i)-min F,  V=2f_n/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2.

The optimum is denoted star, with X_star=g_star=f_star=0. Define the
original residual, for every distinct ordered pair including star, by

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.       (1)

Define the positive affine value reserve

    delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Here are the conclusions, each limited to its stated class.

A. Every nonempty supplied word with min_i h_i>1 has the explicit
two-state, time-dependent Markov construction in Sections 2--5. All
sampled value reserves are positive, the original gradients have dense
signed product correlations and real independent innovations, and every
ordered row (1) is proved. One fixed convex 1-smooth function realizes
the entire run and has unique minimizer 0 and exact initial distance eta.
The normalized payoff and a paid append rule are explicit.

B. For the original recursive diagnostic words H_m, the ENTIRE broader
class of queried Markov product correlations with an aligned initial
point and tight queried optimum upper rows has negligible original-B
payoff, under the varying support-ratio conditions of Section 7. This
allows arbitrary varying positive support ratios, arbitrary correlations,
arbitrary amplitudes, and a completely free terminal gradient and value.
It is not only a bound on the displayed safe rule.

C. The exact small example in Section 9 refutes replacing the distant
rows by adjacent rows, optimum rows, positive affine values, real PSD,
and even all reverse chronological rows. A remote forward row fails.

Neither A nor B nor C is a theorem about unrestricted W_n(h). In
particular min h>1 is already a restriction for A; the additional
geometric hypotheses for B are essential and are identified explicitly.

## 2. The bounded rule, genuine joint law, and real coordinates

Fix n>=1 and h_i>1. Choose two scalar parameters

    1<eta<min_i h_i,  0<theta<1,

and set a bookkeeping value h_n=eta. This h_n is NOT a physical step.
For example, the completely specified choice

    eta=(1+min_i h_i)/2, theta=(n+1)/(n+2)

depends only on the supplied word. Keeping the two scalar parameters
visible makes the continuation law and its bounds clearer. Define

    b_i=(h_i-eta)/eta,
    t_i=theta*min{1,(h_i-eta)/h_(i+1)},
    r_i=-sqrt(t_i/b_i),                         0<=i<n,
    A_0=1, A_(i+1)=sqrt(t_i*b_i)*A_i.                (2)

No Gram entry is a free parameter. The fixed rule reads only successive
physical steps and the two common scalars. Because h_(i+1)>=eta>1,

    0<t_i<=theta<1,
    t_i/b_i<=theta*eta/h_(i+1)<=theta<1,
    h_i-t_i*h_(i+1)>=eta,
    h_i-t_i>=eta.                                   (3)

The third inequality follows from t_i<=(h_i-eta)/h_(i+1).
The fourth follows because h_(i+1)>1. Thus -1<r_i<0.

Let S_0 be a fair sign and let B_0,...,B_(n-1) be independent signs,
independent of S_0, with P(B_i=1)=(1+r_i)/2. Put S_(i+1)=S_i B_i.
The complete finite joint law on sign strings s=(s_0,...,s_n) is

    p(s)=2^(-n-1) product_(i<n)(1+r_i*s_i*s_(i+1)).   (4)

Every atom is positive, and summing successively over the last sign
proves that the probabilities sum to 1. Independent bond signs give

    E[S_i S_j]=product_(k=i)^(j-1)r_k, i<j.          (5)

For example, literal real coordinates u_i(s)=sqrt(p(s))*s_i realize
(5). A smaller explicit realization uses the orthonormal basis
e_0,...,e_n of R^(n+1):

    u_0=e_0,
    u_(i+1)=r_i*u_i+sqrt(1-r_i^2)*e_(i+1).           (6)

Induction gives ||u_i||=1 and (5) as its actual real inner products.
Thus all PSD requirements are proved by coordinates. Every new
direction has a nonzero innovation, and all distinct inner products
are nonzero with sign (-1)^(j-i). The transition law changes when its
successive-step formula changes; it is not a fixed one-initial-sign
constant-correlation law. Section 6 gives a concrete changing example.

Set, in these SAME coordinates,

    g_i=A_i*u_i,
    X_0=eta*u_0,
    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t,
    q_i=A_i^2,
    f_i=(eta-1/2)*q_i, delta_i=(eta-1)*q_i.           (7)

Every q_i and delta_i is strictly positive. These are data at all the
original samples, including the terminal. No subword is reset.

For later use define, for i<=j,

    P_ij=product_(k=i)^(j-1)(-t_k),
    E_ij=product_(k=i)^(j-1)(t_k*b_k)=q_j/q_i,        (8)

with empty products 1. Since A_(k+1)*r_k/A_k=-t_k,

    <g_i,g_j>=q_i*P_ij, i<j.                        (9)

## 3. Actual support and every original directed inequality

First prove the actual support identity

    <X_i,g_i>=eta*q_i, 0<=i<=n.                     (10)

It holds at i=0. The new basis vector e_(i+1) is orthogonal to X_i
and g_i, since both are in span(e_0,...,e_i). Hence

    <X_(i+1),u_(i+1)>=r_i*(eta-h_i)*A_i
                           =eta*A_(i+1),

where (2) was used. This proves (10) by induction. It proves support
along the actual GD displacement; it is not an independently assigned
inner product.

The optimum rows are now exactly

    Q_(i,star)=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-(eta-1)*q_i<0.                       (11)

For every i<j, all future innovations in u_j are orthogonal to X_i,
so <X_i,g_j>=eta*<g_i,g_j>. Expanding (1), (7), and (10) gives

    Q_(j,i)=(eta-1)*q_i*(P_ij-1).                   (12)

If j-i is odd then P_ij<0. If j-i is even, 0<P_ij<1 by (3).
Thus every reverse chronological row is strictly negative.

For the other orientation keep the whole physical displacement:

    Q_(i,j)/q_i
      =eta-sum_(k=i)^(j-1)h_k*P_ik-P_ij
                                      -(eta-1)*E_ij. (13)

No old step is omitted from this expression. We show

    sum_(k=i)^(j-1)h_k*P_ik+P_ij>=eta.              (14)

For even d=j-i, pair the physical terms consecutively:

    sum_(k=i)^(j-1)h_k*P_ik+P_ij
      =sum_(r=0)^(d/2-1)P_(i,i+2r)
          [h_(i+2r)-t_(i+2r)*h_(i+2r+1)]+P_ij.

Each even-prefix product is positive. Every bracket is at least eta
by (3), and the first product is 1. The last term P_ij is positive,
which proves (14).

For odd d=2v+1, the analogous identity is

    sum_(r=0)^(v-1)P_(i,i+2r)
          [h_(i+2r)-t_(i+2r)*h_(i+2r+1)]
        +P_(i,j-1)*[h_(j-1)-t_(j-1)].

If v=0, the only bracket is at least eta by (3). If v>=1,
the first paired bracket is at least eta and every remaining term
is positive. This proves (14) in every case. Equation (13) is
therefore at most -(eta-1)*E_ij<0.

Equations (11)--(14) prove ALL ordered original rows, including every
old/new cross row and both directions at the unqueried terminal.

## 4. One static function and the true radius

Define y_i=X_i-g_i and form, once for the complete supplied word,

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,max_(0<=i<=n)(<y_i,v>-delta_i)},
    F(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H(v)).                          (15)

This is the explicit full-data conjugate of accepted
`1252b8823bb5a290`. To match its notation, expand

    <X_i,g_i>-f_i+<X_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2
       =||v||^2/2+<X_i-g_i,v>-delta_i.

The established all-pair inequalities therefore imply that F is
everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, and that
F(X_i)=f_i and grad F(X_i)=g_i for EVERY i. Consequently its actual
GD visits precisely the prescribed samples. The function is fixed
before the first update.

Every delta_i is strictly positive. The maximum in (15) therefore
equals 0 in a neighborhood of v=0. Since H(v)>=||v||^2/2, F(0)=0.
For any X!=0 choose a sufficiently small s in (0,1) with sX in that
neighborhood. Then

    F(X)>=(s-s^2/2)||X||^2>0.

Thus argmin F={0}, and the true initial squared distance is exactly
||X_0||^2=eta^2. It has not been replaced by a projected or reset radius.
The exact payoff is

    V=(2eta-1)/eta^2 * product_(i<n)(t_i*b_i).        (16)

Every queried i>=1 produces actual paid transverse action. The point
X_i has zero e_i coordinate, whereas the new coordinate of g_i is
A_i*sqrt(1-r_(i-1)^2)>0. Thus the e_i component of X_(i+1)-X_i is

    -h_i*A_i*sqrt(1-r_(i-1)^2),                     (17)

which is nonzero. In particular a two-step segment already has a
nonzero displacement transverse to its first gradient. All of it is
inside (7), (10), (13), and the distance calculation above.

For arbitrary smoothness L>0 use L*F and physical steps h_i/L; the
same normalized W=2R payoff (16) follows.

## 5. The paid continuation rule and its limit

For a jointly planned chronological segment [r,s), the amplitude and
value transfer is exactly

    q_s=q_r*E_rs, f_s=f_r*E_rs,
    X_s=X_r-sum_(i=r)^(s-1)h_i*A_i*u_i.              (18)

The direction is propagated by (6), keeping the old coordinates.
The state consists of the actual X_r,u_r,A_r, the common eta, and the
last physical-step guard; no independent Gram block or free mode is
chosen. The vector dimension grows by the prescribed orthogonal
innovation. Its coefficient is fixed by (2).

For a literal append to already fixed jets 0,...,k, the sufficient
join condition inherited from (3) is

    t_(k-1)*h_k<=h_(k-1)-eta,                       (19)

when k>=1. Here h_k is the first NEW physical step. Retain all old
jets and transition coefficients, require h_k>eta, and choose every
new transition by (2) using the supplied future word. All old guards
then remain true and (19) supplies the missing boundary guard.
The proofs of (10)--(14) apply to every pair straddling the boundary.

This proves existence of a single interpolant for the extended full
history. It does NOT mean changing the running objective at a join,
nor that the old interpolant (15) on a shorter data set already has
the required behavior outside its samples. In the intended use the
entire word, all guards and (15) are fixed before GD starts.

The old artificial value h_k=eta used to terminate a shorter word
cannot simply be replaced for free by a larger physical h_k. If (19)
fails, this sufficient append rule gives no extension claim. The
exact all-pair formula (13) remains available, but adjacent constraints
alone cannot repair it, as Section 9 demonstrates.

For perspective, even the safe family itself is extremely weak on
the original H_m of Section 8. Put ell=1+a. Every even physical index
has h_i=ell; there are N/2 such steps and the last step is even.
Let b_low=(ell-eta)/eta<a. Pair each low step except the last with
the following step H>ell. Equations (2) give

    (t_low*b_low)*(t_high*b_high)
      <=theta^2*b_low^2*(H-eta)/H<=b_low^2.

The unpaired last step has t_last=theta*b_low and contributes
theta*b_low^2<=b_low^2, since b_low<a<1. The prefactor in (16) is
at most 1 because eta^2-(2eta-1)=(eta-1)^2. Thus

    V<=b_low^N<a^N.                                 (20)

This is only the displayed guard rule. The next result treats a much
larger family, without imposing that guard or a constant eta.

## 6. Concrete legal changing segment

For h=(3,10), choose eta=2 and theta=1/2. Then

    b=(1/2,4), t=(1/20,1/2),
    r=(-1/sqrt(10),-1/(2sqrt(2))),
    q=(1,1/40,1/20), f=(3/2,3/80,3/40).

One exact real realization is

    g_0=e_0,
    g_1=(-e_0+3e_1)/20,
    g_2=(e_0-3e_1+sqrt(70)e_2)/40,
    X_0=2e_0, X_1=-e_0, X_2=-(e_0+3e_1)/2.

These are actual positions from the stated steps. The reserves are
delta=(1,1/40,1/20). All six non-optimum ordered residuals are

    Q_01=-39/40, Q_10=-21/20,
    Q_02=-23/40, Q_20=-39/40,
    Q_12=-19/80, Q_21=-3/80.

The optimum rows are (11). Thus the fixed function (15) realizes
the segment, has unique minimizer 0, true squared distance 4, and
normalized payoff V=3/80. Its second displacement has e_1 component
-3/2, and its two transition correlations are different. This is a
complete legal coupled segment, not a correlation-only example.
It is not claimed stronger than the older tests on this word.

## 7. A whole-class obstruction with varying positive affine values

The following hypothesis class is substantially broader than (2).
Let an ACTUAL convex 1-smooth function with minimum 0 at 0 generate
a nonzero queried trajectory g_i=A_i*u_i, A_i>0, ||u_i||=1,
for 0<=i<n. Suppose:

  (i) The initial point is X_0=eta_0*g_0.
 (ii) For every queried i there is 1<eta_i<h_i such that

          <X_i,g_i>=eta_i*q_i,
          f_i=(eta_i-1/2)*q_i, q_i=A_i^2.           (21)

(iii) There are real r_0,...,r_(n-2) in [-1,1] with

          <u_i,u_j>=product_(k=i)^(j-1)r_k, i<j<n. (22)

There is NO constraint on the terminal gradient or value beyond
being generated by the same legal function. No sufficient guards,
stationarity, common amplitude ratio, common eta, or full-support
probability assumption is imposed on this class.

First, the true initial distance is exactly eta_0*A_0. Indeed (21)
at i=0 implies f_0+q_0/2=eta_0*q_0. For any minimizer z, the
necessary smooth interpolation row gives

    f_0+q_0/2<=<g_0,X_0-z>=eta_0*q_0-<g_0,z>.

Hence <g_0,z><=0 and
||X_0-z||^2=eta_0^2*q_0-2eta_0<g_0,z>+||z||^2
>=eta_0^2*q_0. The minimizer 0 attains this distance. Thus the
denominator is proved even when the minimizer is not unique.

The Markov product identity and X_i in span(g_0,...,g_i) give

    <X_(i+1),u_(i+1)>=r_i*(eta_i-h_i)*A_i.

Combining with (21) at i+1 forces r_i<0 and gives

    b_i=(h_i-eta_i)/eta_(i+1)>0,
    A_(i+1)=-r_i*b_i*A_i,
    t_i=r_i^2*b_i>0,
    q_(i+1)=t_i*b_i*q_i,       0<=i<n-1.            (23)

The b_i in this section are the varying-ratio generalization of (2),
not an assumption that eta_i is constant. For i<j<n, (22) also gives
<X_i,g_j>=eta_i<g_i,g_j>. Full expansion of the ORIGINAL row yields

    Q_(j,i)=(eta_i-1)*q_i
             [product_(k=i)^(j-1)(-t_k)-1].         (24)

All occurrences of eta_j cancel in this identity. Thus for any two
queried indices of the same parity,

    product_(k=i)^(j-1)t_k<=1.                      (25)

This is a necessary remote-row restriction, irrespective of how the
correlations and support ratios are selected from the full word.
It is NOT asserted sufficient for interpolation.

If n is odd, its last queried index n-1 is even. Taking (i,j)=(0,n-1)
in (25), or using the empty product for n=1, gives

    q_(n-1)/q_0<=product_(i=0)^(n-2)b_i
                        <=product_(i=0)^(n-2)(h_i-1). (26)

The last inequality follows individually from eta_i>1 and
eta_(i+1)>1. Thus no fixed positive floor eta_i-1 is needed; the
bound remains uniform when every reserve ratio tends to zero.

For a final physical step ell=h_(n-1) in (1,2), smoothness gives

    2f_n<=2f_(n-1)-ell*(2-ell)*q_(n-1)
          =[2(eta_(n-1)-1)+(ell-1)^2]*q_(n-1)
          <[(ell-1)^2+2(ell-1)]*q_(n-1).           (27)

This uses the actual terminal value, without specifying its gradient
or requiring any terminal analogue of (21). Equations (26)--(27)
are the complete-class obstruction used next.

## 8. Exact comparison on the ORIGINAL enclosing-m words

For m>=4 define

    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m,
    c=sigma*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.     (28)

The word is exactly h=H_m, with the SAME enclosing m in every child.
Its smallest and last step is ell=1+a, and 0<a<1/2, so ell<2.
The offset product is

    D_m:=product_(h_i in H_m)(h_i-1)
        =a^(N-1)*c^(N-m-1)
        =beta^(N-1)*c^(-m).                         (29)

One direct proof is induction from D_0=1 and
D_(j+1)=D_j^2*a*c^j. The displayed expression at general j is
a^(2^j-1)c^(2^j-j-1). Thus the product through the last queried
transition, omitting the final physical step, is D_m/a.

For EVERY legal instance in Section 7, divide (27) by the true
eta_0^2*q_0 and apply (26) and (29). It follows that

    V <= (a+2)*beta^(N-1)*c^(-m).                   (30)

The strict version follows when n>=1 and the ratios in (21) are
strict, but the weak inequality is the convenient uniform statement.
It holds uniformly over arbitrary horizon-dependent correlations,
amplitudes, functions, dimensions and eta_i satisfying the class
hypotheses. The terminal is entirely free.

Let B_n and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n be the ORIGINAL all-split denominator and
benchmark in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`. That fact gives
B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<1+4*3^m. Since beta^(N-1)<=exp(-(N-1)/m),

    B_n*V <= (a+2)*(1+4*3^m)*c^(-m)*exp(-(2^m-1)/m)
             ->0.                                  (31)

For completeness, c>2 and a+2<5/2 bound the logarithm of all factors
except the final exponential by a constant plus m*log(3/2).
The quantity 2^m/m divided by m tends to infinity. This proves the
limit without a numerical evaluation or a benchmark replacement.

Equation (31) closes this precise class as a source of a matching
lower witness on the original diagnostic words. It does NOT show
small unrestricted W_n(H_m), and it does NOT decide the original
all-schedule exact-profile Goal.

## 9. Exact failure of local-only interpolation checks

Take the physical word h=(3,10) and eta=2, but now choose the legal
Markov correlations r_0=-1/sqrt(2), r_1=-1/2. Their corresponding
parameters in (23) are b=(1/2,4), t=(1/4,1). They have t_0*t_1=1/4,
so even the same-parity reverse-row condition holds. Use

    g_0=e_0,
    g_1=(-e_0+e_1)/4,
    g_2=(e_0-e_1)/4+sqrt(3/8)*e_2,
    X_0=2e_0, X_1=-e_0, X_2=(3e_0-5e_1)/2,
    q=(1,1/8,1/2), f=(3/2,3/16,3/4).

These are literal real coordinates, with every innovation nonzero,
and every sample has <X_i,g_i>=2q_i and delta_i=q_i>0.
All optimum rows pass. The adjacent rows are

    Q_01=-7/8, Q_10=-5/4,
    Q_12=-11/8, Q_21=-1/4.

The remote reverse row also passes: Q_20=-3/4. But

    Q_02=3/4>0.                                    (32)

For a direct check, f_0-f_2=3/4,
<g_0,X_2-X_0>=-1/2, and ||g_0-g_2||^2=1.
Thus (32) follows from (1). There is no static convex 1-smooth
interpolant for these jets. This is a counterexample ONLY to the
local-only sufficiency claim, not a legal GD counterexample to any
theorem about actual functions.

## 10. The remaining interface and a priced escape identity

Changing eta_i alone does not escape (30) while the other hypotheses
of Section 7 hold. The crucial conditions are aligned initial support,
the Markov product relation, h_i>eta_i>1, and tight optimum upper rows.
Actual transverse displacement, positive affine values and varying
dependence have all been included in the excluded class.

One precise escape is to retain nonzero optimum slack. Define

    z_i=<X_i,g_i>-f_i-q_i/2>=0,
    eta_i=<X_i,g_i>/q_i,
    f_i=(eta_i-1/2)*q_i-z_i.                         (33)

Under the same aligned-anchor Markov support calculation, the actual
reverse row becomes, by expanding its value difference,

    Q_(j,i)=(eta_i-1)*q_i
             [product_(k=i)^(j-1)(-t_k)-1]+z_i-z_j. (34)

Positive reserves require z_i<(eta_i-1)*q_i. Thus it is invalid to
keep the product amplification while dropping its cost z_j in the
terminal value. Another possible escape introduces an initial anchor
with correlations to future innovations, in which case the support
recursion (23) and (24) acquire additional terms. Allowing support
ratios to cross physical step sizes also changes the sign/parity
argument. None of these broader interfaces is closed by this draft.

The next round should preserve the actual law, every old/new row,
the true distance and the slack cost in (33)--(34). A generic free
Gram matrix or a finite prescribed-word success would not close the
arbitrary-h quantifier gap. No new computational or verifier allowance
is inferred from this candidate.

## 11. Literature and proof-dependency audit

The final memory audit also recovered the older UNVERIFIED public
findings `c035d2edbaffb2c0` and `fd7036b719ef9645`. Their full domains
and coordinate definitions were read. The former treats first
occurrences of consecutive ruler levels, with constant gradients in
the intervening ordinary blocks and a common queried terminal cell;
it already includes positive heights and free affine intercepts in
that domain. The latter prices a negative-then-positive fresh relay.
Those are relevant prior boundaries, not premises of this manuscript.

C97 instead changes gradients at EVERY actual physical sample and
leaves the entire final completion free in its class bound. Its
restriction 1<eta_i<h_i and tight optimum upper rows is stronger in
a different direction. We do not claim that positive affine Markov
relays themselves are new, or that C97 subsumes either older family.
Future slack or mixed-sign work must compare its complete domain with
those older interfaces before claiming an escape.

Matlas searches for nonstationary binary Markov product correlations
returned Gaussian graph, noncommutative exchangeability and resistance
distance results, none applicable to the interpolation problem. The
first built-in web attempt returned: "Fatal error: connection failed:
error sending request". A later search located Tuncer Kaya, *An exact
alternative solution method of 1D Ising model with Block-spin
transformation at H=0*, arXiv:1801.04452 (2018). The exact PDF was
downloaded to downloads/ising_binary_bonds_1801.04452.pdf; the browser
extraction is saved alongside it and Sections I--II and the displayed
derivation through the correlation recursion were read.

That paper concerns spin correlations for an Ising chain. Those
statements do not establish any GD interpolation inequality, positive
value reserve, minimizer distance, or prescribed-word payoff. We use
no external theorem or proof assertion from it. The finite independent-
bond law (4), its normalization and its product moments are proved
directly above. Sections 3--10 likewise use only the two accepted
predecessors named at the start and their fully displayed new algebra.
This is one joint unverified manuscript, not a chain of unverified
intermediate facts.

# Actual chronological transport and a complete-prefix geometric mixture

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Project gd-exact-profile-20260926, worker max4,
invocation82. Steering: cb75d819e7b0002c. Plan: 30ed74753a6f5e0a.
No fact submission is authorized or made; no new fact_id exists.

Two mechanisms are screened completely within their stated scopes.

1. An explicit chronological transport uses actual middle-to-middle
   interpolation rows, with nonnegative coefficients and separate value
   balance at every original node. Its entire original-space quadratic
   is derived. A negative formal direction also excludes the larger
   class of all backward-only prefix transports with outgoing optimum
   rows and the unchanged last-leaf terminal row.
2. A geometric mixture uses every prefix anchor with physical-step
   occupation weights and optimizes its mixing with the optimum by an
   explicit scalar formula. A legal scalar quadratic on every SAME-m
   diagnostic hierarchy violates the proposed pointwise bound for this
   mixture. Its actual objective is nevertheless below the target.

These conclusions are draft findings, not verified facts. Neither is a
full-function upper bound or a disproof of such an upper bound. The
original unrestricted minimax target remains OPEN.

All new ingredients are derived jointly below. The earlier fan report
3562fdffe39dd0f4 is awareness, not a premise. Accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 is used for the original interpolation orientation,
the maximal complete-prefix extension, and its terminal-square
interface. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is used only at its stated benchmark
and hierarchy scope. No external mathematical theorem is imported.

## 1. Original model, same hierarchy, and symbolic bounds

For an arbitrary nonnegative word h of length n, normalize to smoothness
one and a specified minimizer x_star=0, f_star=0. The original iterates
and interpolation residuals are

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Every legal transcript has Q_ij<=0 for all ordered distinct pairs,
including the optimum. The objective is W=2R: a certificate at level
chi must bound 2f_n/||x_0||^2 in every finite dimension. In a full
quadratic certificate all original formal gradients remain independent.

For the diagnostic family fix any integer m>=1536 ONCE and put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum h, D=1+2S,
    R=n-1, K=n-2, I={1,...,K}.

Thus the requested interface has singleton first sector0, all
independent middle gradients indexed by I, and singleton last queried
sectorR. No physical query or last leaf is removed. Let

    A=h_0=h_R=1+a,
    J=2^(m-1)-1,
    t=a*c^(m-1), T=h_J=1+t,
    s=sum H_(m-1).

Here J is the actual root-step index, S=2s+T, and the suffix strictly
after J has mass s. Throughout this report T is the root step, whereas
t is that step minus one. These scalar symbols are local to this
report; they do not replace any project benchmark capacity.

The following loose bounds suffice, and require no numerical
evaluation:

    12/5<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2, 1<A<3/2.

For the first bounds, sqrt(2)>7/5 gives lambda>12/5, while
sqrt(2)<10/7 and epsilon<=1/6144 give
c<(17/7)(6145/6144)<5/2. Also beta>99/100, so
a>(99/100)/(5/2)=99/250>1/3. Finally
a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2.

For completeness the exact hierarchy mass can be obtained symbolically.
Writing S_j=sum H_j and v=a/(c-2), literal concatenation gives

    1+S_j=(1-v)2^j+v*c^j.

The formula has the required initial value and satisfies
1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j because v(c-2)=a. Moreover
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, so 0<v<1. Consequently,

    S_j<c^j for j>=1,
    S/T<c/a<15/2,
    t>c^(m-1)/3>17, T>18.

All steps exceed one, so S>n and D>2^(m+1)-1. In particular,

    chi_target=17/(16D)<2^(-m).

The final strict inequality follows from
2^(m+1)-1>(17/16)2^m for m>=1. This establishes every parameter
comparison used below at every allowed m without executing a recurrence.

## 2. Mechanism1: an explicit original chronological transport

Choose the following literal formulas:

    b_i=h_i/S                    (0<=i<=R),
    F_i=(sum_(t=0)^i h_t)/S      (0<=i<R).

Use the half-multiplier convention lambda_ij=2mu_ij, and set

    mu_(i,star)=b_i              (0<=i<=R),
    mu_(i+1,i)=F_i               (0<=i<R),
    mu_(n,R)=1.

Every other multiplier is zero. In particular there are actual
middle-to-middle rows Q_(i+1,i) for 1<=i<K. This is not a fan through
one algebraic middle mean. The coefficients depend on the physical
chronological masses, including the actual long dyadic steps.

All coefficients are nonnegative. They are finite sums with a positive
denominator, not a recursively evaluated or implicitly defined law.
Their signs therefore close at every depth of the stated hierarchy.

The value balance is separate at every original node. At0 it is
b_0-F_0=0. At every 1<=i<R it is

    b_i+F_(i-1)-F_i=0.

At R, outgoing mass is b_R+F_(R-1)=1 and incoming terminal mass is1.
At n, the outgoing mass is1. Thus sum mu_ij Q_ij has value coefficient
one at f_n and zero at every other finite f_i. The optimum value is
fixed to zero. No equality between intermediate sampled values is used.

Define the one global quadratic

    C_chi=chi||x_0||^2-2f_n+2sum mu_ij Q_ij.

Only this one initial/common charge is present. We now expand the
entire polynomial, rather than testing just its collapsed sector.

## 3. Entire transport remainder and the actual free-terminal square

Put

    g_b=sum_(i=0)^R b_i g_i,
    w=A+1/2-A^2/2.

Directly from the actual final update x_n=x_R-A g_R,

    Q_(n,R)=f_n-f_R+(A-A^2/2)||g_R||^2
                         +||g_n+(A-1)g_R||^2/2.

This identity retains the free formal terminal g_n. Expanding the
other original rows gives the exact full-space identity

    C_chi=chi||x_0||^2-2<g_b,x_0>+2K_ch
                              +||g_n+(A-1)g_R||^2,          (3.1)

where

    K_ch=sum_(i=0)^(R-1) F_i||g_i||^2+w||g_R||^2
       +sum_(0<=i<j<=R) h_i*b_j <g_i,g_j>
       +sum_(i=0)^(R-1) F_i(h_i-1)<g_i,g_(i+1)>.          (3.2)

Here is an independent coefficient check. The optimum rows are

    Q_(i,star)=f_i-<g_i,x_0>
                  +sum_(t<i)h_t<g_i,g_t>+||g_i||^2/2.

The transport rows are

    Q_(i+1,i)=f_(i+1)-f_i+(h_i-1)<g_(i+1),g_i>
                             +(||g_i||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)/2.

For i<R the combined half-diagonal is
(b_i+F_(i-1)+F_i)/2=F_i, with F_(-1)=0. At R it is
(b_R+F_(R-1))/2+A-A^2/2=w. The optimum-row chronological
cross terms and the adjacent-row cross terms are precisely the two
sums in (3.2). This accounts for every term and both middle endpoints.

No indefinite storage is suppressed: K_ch is the full remainder,
and its sign is still to be tested. In particular, positive transport
diagonals do not by themselves prove it positive semidefinite.

### Exact relationship with the former signed variance problem

Let P_i=sum_(l=1)^(i-1)h_l be the middle mass before i, let
B=sum_(i in I)h_i, and let d_I=sum_(i in I)h_i g_i. Set the actual
outer formal vectors x_0=g_0=g_R=g_n=0 only to display the middle
principal form of (3.2). Since b_i=h_i/S, it is exactly

    K_I=||d_I||^2/(2S)
        +sum_(i=1)^K d_i||g_i||^2
        +sum_(i=1)^(K-1) e_i<g_i,g_(i+1)>,                 (3.3)

    d_i=[A+P_i+h_i(2-h_i)/2]/S,
    e_i=F_i(h_i-1).

Indeed the all-pairs chronological sum in (3.2) is
[||d_I||^2-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2]/(2S). Equation (3.3) shows
exactly the attempted payment: the signed h_i(2-h_i)/(2S)
diagonal is supplemented by the positive cumulative-mass term
(A+P_i)/S, but this payment introduces the full adjacent bilinear
load e_i<g_i,g_(i+1)>. It is not an unpriced PSD correction.

To see explicitly that no mean/chronology term has been discarded,
write only algebraically

    q=d_I/B, nu_i=g_i-q, sum h_i nu_i=0.

Then (3.3) is exactly

    [B^2/(2S)+sum d_i+sum e_i]||q||^2
    +<q,2sum d_i nu_i+sum e_i(nu_i+nu_(i+1))>
    +sum d_i||nu_i||^2+sum e_i<nu_i,nu_(i+1)>.             (3.4)

The d_i sums run over I, and the e_i sums over1<=i<K. With
J2=sum h_i^2 nu_i, the linear diagonal part is

    2sum d_i nu_i
       =(2/S)sum (A+P_i)nu_i-J2/S,

because sum h_i nu_i=0. Thus the squared-step/mean cross term
survives explicitly. Chronological order is carried by each P_i and
by each actual adjacent pair, not replaced by a displacement mean.
The old fan's altered mean and Z_chron coefficients are replaced by
these displayed coefficients because the row law itself has changed;
they have not been assumed zero. On the full space, (3.2) also retains
the boundary loads F_0(h_0-1)<g_0,g_1> and
F_K(h_K-1)<g_K,g_R>, together with every optimum cross term.
There is no queried value, jet, or interpolation row assigned to q.

The sign question therefore concerns all of (3.1), not a variance
subblock. The following direction decides that question negatively.

## 4. A parameter-uniform negative direction, and a larger support obstruction

Fix a nonzero Euclidean vector z. Set x_0=0 and assign the ORIGINAL
formal gradients by

    g_i=0               for 0<=i<J,
    g_J=(t/2)z,
    g_i=-z              for J<i<=R,
    g_n=a z.

For i>J define u_i=sum_(J<l<i)h_l. These are the exact intervening
physical masses. The positions are

    x_i=0                         for i<=J,
    x_i=[-T*t/2+u_i]z             for i>J,
    x_n=[-T*t/2+s]z.

To evaluate the original rows, assign formal values

    f_i=0                                      for i<J,
    f_J=-(t^2/8)||z||^2,
    f_i=[t^2/4-1/2-u_i]||z||^2                 for J<i<=R,
    f_n=[t^2/4-1/2-s+A^2/2]||z||^2.                         (4.1)

These are deliberately FORMAL data, not a legal function. Values can
be used to test a flow-balanced polynomial because all their
coefficients cancel before PSD is tested.

Every backward prefix row and every outgoing prefix-optimum row is
nonpositive. A complete check, not only a neighboring check, is:

* If both finite indices are before J, the residual is0.
* If the source is J and the target is before J or star, the residual
  is -t^2/8+(t/2)^2/2=0, times ||z||^2.
* If the source i is after J and the target is J, the residual is0.
  Substitution gives
  t^2/4-1/2+t^2/8-T*t/2+(1+t/2)^2/2=0.
* If the source is after J and the target is before J or star, the
  residual is -(t^2/4+t/2)||z||^2.
* If both source and target are after J and source is later, the
  gradients agree and the chronological value/position differences
  cancel, giving residual0.

The last physical step gives Q_(n,R)=0 by (4.1). Its actual terminal
square vanishes, since g_n+(A-1)g_R=a z-a z=0.

The assigned f_n is strictly positive for every allowed m. Put
y=c^(m-1). Then t>y/3, s<y, and y>72. Therefore

    t^2/4>y^2/36>2y>y+1/2>s+1/2.

Equation (4.1) implies f_n>0 even without its positive A^2/2 term.
For the explicit transport of Section2, all backward adjacent rows
vanish, while the outgoing optimum rows after J have total weight
s/S. Its COMPLETE certificate is consequently

    C_chi=-2f_n-2(s/S)(t^2/4+t/2)||z||^2<0.                 (4.2)

This works for EVERY finite chi because x_0=0. It therefore refutes
full PSD even at the allowed17/(16D) level or any larger level.

The same calculation proves a strictly larger support obstruction.
Allow ANY nonnegative prefix multipliers supported on

    Q_(i,star), 0<=i<=R,
    Q_(i,j), 0<=j<i<=R,

with arbitrary separate node balance, and keep mu_(n,R)=1 as the
only terminal row. All allowed rows are nonpositive at (4.1), and
x_0=0, f_n>0. Thus every such full certificate is negative there.
This excludes arbitrary nonlocal backward transports too, not merely
the particular nearest-neighbor weights of Section2. No sign choice
or extra initial charge can repair this stated support class.

This direction is NOT a primal escape. The original incoming optimum
row at J is

    Q_(star,J)=-f_J+||g_J||^2/2=(t^2/4)||z||^2>0.

Likewise Q_(0,J)>0, since x_J=x_0=0 and g_0=0. These omitted
original constraints are mandatory for a legal function. A successful
certificate can price them with additional support; the obstruction
does not exclude forward prefix rows, optimum-to-query rows, or a
different terminal distribution. It does not exclude the full
function-class upper bound. No unchanged fan or new fixed three-sector
classification has been used as a mechanism here.

## 5. Mechanism2: complete-prefix variational formula and explicit law

We now use a different terminal distribution, after the decisive
failure of Mechanism1. For ANY legal original prefix let

    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2  (0<=i<n),
    y_star=delta_star=0,
    J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}.

The original optimum rows give delta_i>=0. Accepted1252 constructs
the maximal legal prefix extension from

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in J_pre)(<y_i,v>-delta_i),
    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).

For a probability vector alpha on ALL of J_pre define

    Psi(alpha)=2sum_i alpha_i delta_i
                     +||x_n-sum_i alpha_i y_i||^2.          (5.1)

The exact complete-prefix identity is

    2F(x_n)=min_(alpha in simplex(J_pre)) Psi(alpha).         (5.2)

Here is a direct full-dimensional derivation of the variational step.
For every alpha the maximum defining H dominates its alpha-average.
Completing the square in v gives 2F(x_n)<=Psi(alpha). The finite
simplex is compact, so a minimizer alpha^o exists. Let
V=x_n-sum alpha^o_i y_i. The derivative of Psi/2 with respect to
alpha_i is delta_i-<V,y_i>. Feasible exchanges of probability show
that this derivative equals a common number d on positive coordinates
and is at least d on all remaining coordinates. Hence

    max_i(<y_i,V>-delta_i)=-d
      =sum_i alpha^o_i(<y_i,V>-delta_i).

Evaluation of <x_n,V>-H(V) now gives
||V||^2/2+sum alpha^o_i delta_i=Psi(alpha^o)/2. This proves the
reverse inequality in (5.2) in arbitrary dimension. It neither
maximizes over rank-one Gram matrices nor discards prefix constraints.

The explicit candidate distribution is the following. Keep ALL
original finite anchors in their physical occupation barycenter:

    omega_i=h_i/S,
    y_bar=sum_(i=0)^R omega_i y_i,
    delta_bar=sum_(i=0)^R omega_i delta_i.

Mix this entire barycenter with the optimum. If y_bar!=0 set

    theta=clip_[0,1]((<x_n,y_bar>-delta_bar)/||y_bar||^2),

and if y_bar=0 set theta=0. Define

    alpha_star=1-theta, alpha_i=theta*omega_i.

This is an explicit geometry-dependent probability law, with no
unknown optimization, no hidden PSD block, and no recursive execution.
It is exactly the minimizer of (5.1) on this particular segment:

    Psi_occ=||x_n||^2
      +2theta(delta_bar-<x_n,y_bar>)+theta^2||y_bar||^2.      (5.3)

The zero-barycenter convention is minimizing because delta_bar>=0.
At no point is (5.2) presumed losslessly reducible to this segment.

The proposed target inequality for this rule is stated before any
comparison:

    Psi_occ <= [17/(16D)]||x_0||^2                           (5.4)

for EVERY legal original prefix of the SAME-m H_m, with its specified
minimizer0. If (5.4) held, then every legal continuation would obey
2f_n<=2F(x_n)<=Psi_occ and the required full W bound would follow.
The next section shows that (5.4) is false, by an actual full function.

## 6. A legal scalar quadratic discriminator on every same-m hierarchy

Choose the literal curvature

    kappa=2/T,
    f(x)=kappa*x^2/2 on the real line,
    x_0=1.

Since T>18, 0<kappa<1. Thus f is convex, differentiable and
1-smooth, its complete minimum set is {0}, and its true initial
radius is1. The original GD equations are exactly

    g_i=kappa*x_i,
    x_(i+1)=(1-kappa*h_i)x_i,
    x_n=P=product_(i=0)^R (1-kappa*h_i).

This is a symbolic product identity; no trajectory or product has
been executed or numerically evaluated. All original rows, including
terminal and optimum rows, are satisfied. In fact for any pair of
points of this quadratic,

    Q_ij=-kappa(1-kappa)(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0.                   (6.1)

At the whole root J the multiplier is 1-kappa*T=-1. At every other
physical index i, the largest possible step is the child-root step
1+t/c, so with z_i=kappa*h_i,

    0<z_i<=2(1+t/c)/(1+t)<9/10.                            (6.2)

For the last bound use c>12/5 to replace 2/c by5/6, and then
2+(5/6)t<(9/10)(1+t) because t>17>33/2. Therefore every
non-root multiplier is strictly positive. There is exactly ONE sign
change in the actual scalar iterates and

    P<0.

The complete physical occupation barycenter has an exact telescoping
formula; it does not rely on an approximation to this product:

    sum_i h_i*x_i=(1-P)/kappa,
    y_bar=(1-kappa)(1-P)/(kappa*S)>0,
    delta_bar=[kappa(1-kappa)/(2S)]sum_i h_i*x_i^2>0.

The first equation is the sum of the literal original GD updates.
Since x_n=P<0, it follows that
<x_n,y_bar>-delta_bar<0. The explicit optimal theta in Section5
is consequently0, and

    Psi_occ=P^2.                                            (6.3)

This failure is not an unexplained omission of anchors: the
whole physical average points in the wrong direction for this legal
terminal geometry. Every theta>0 increases (5.3) at its starting end.

### Uniform lower bound on the surrogate, with no numerical product

From (6.2), log(1-z)>=-10z for 0<=z<=9/10; this follows by
integrating -1/(1-z) from0 to z and bounding the integrand by10.
Moreover

    sum_(i!=J) z_i=2S/T-2<13.

Thus

    log|P|=sum_(i!=J)log(1-z_i)>-130,
    Psi_occ=P^2>exp(-260).                                  (6.4)

Since log2>1/2 (integrate1/u on[1,2]), m>=1536 gives

    chi_target<2^(-m)<exp(-m/2)<=exp(-768)<exp(-260).

Combining this with (6.3)--(6.4) proves that (5.4) fails for EVERY
integer m>=1536. All parameters and every physical step are those of
the original hierarchy. No child parameter, terminal, or normalization
has changed.

### The actual objective is below the target, not a primal escape

Both immediate child-root steps occur physically. At either such
step, z_i=2(1+t/c)/(1+t)>2/c>4/5, so its positive multiplier is
less than1/5. Every other non-root multiplier is between0 and1,
and the whole-root multiplier has magnitude1. Therefore

    P^2<1/625.

Also D/T=1/T+2S/T<16. The function's actual normalized score is
2f(x_n)=kappa*P^2, and hence

    D*kappa*P^2<32/625<1/16.

The final comparison is the exact integer inequality512<625.
Consequently this legal function has actual payoff LESS than
1/(16D), even though the proposed mixture upper surrogate exceeds
17/(16D). This is a failure of the declared distribution rule only.
It gives no lower-bound obstruction to the full-class threshold.

## 7. An explicit bound on the anchor effect lost by the occupation average

To quantify why the segment is not lossless on the preceding legal
example, inspect the actual last prefix anchor R already contained
in the complete list. This is a diagnostic within the same example,
not a third mechanism or a proposed universal last-leaf proof.

Let b=1-kappa*A. Since T>18 and A<3/2, 1/2<b<1, and

    x_R=P/b<0,
    y_R=(1-kappa)x_R<0,
    delta_R=kappa(1-kappa)x_R^2/2.

The two-coordinate vector supported at star and this ACTUAL R has
the explicitly minimizing weight

    eta_R=[1-kappa(A+1/2)]/(1-kappa),
    0<eta_R<1.

The numerator is positive because kappa(A+1/2)<4/T<1; the strict
upper bound follows because A+1/2>1. Substitution in the SAME full
functional (5.1) gives

    Psi_R=P^2-[P-(kappa/2)x_R]^2
         =[kappa*b-kappa^2/4]x_R^2
         <kappa*x_R^2<4kappa*P^2.

Therefore

    min_(full simplex) Psi <= Psi_R <128/(625D)<1/(4D).

The last strict comparison again follows from512<625. Thus the full
anchor problem on this legal example has an upper bound below the
target, whereas the fixed-relative occupation mixture has an upper
bound bounded below by exp(-260). The missing original anchor effect
has been exhibited and quantitatively bounded. There is no assertion
that the last anchor is optimal for the full simplex or sufficient
for other legal transcripts.

Geometrically, the original post-root anchors include negative y_i;
their positive weights in the full occupation average are overwhelmed
by the earlier anchors, because the exact weighted average is
proportional to1-P. A scalar rescaling of that average cannot recover
the negative direction. A future full-prefix rule would need to change
the relative weights using the actual geometry, with a pointwise proof
covering every legal transcript. No such new rule is begun here.

## 8. Literature grounding and method

Memory retrieval included the applicable steering, the prior complete
middle-lift report3562fdffe39dd0f4 and its scoped dead end, and fact
searches for the original terminal interface. Narrow searches also
checked for earlier chronological-transport and occupation-mixture
obstructions. No recalled unverified finding is a proof premise.

The direct-proving, construct-counterexamples, and identify-key-failures
procedures were used to screen the two laws and distinguish the two
types of discriminator. No verifier claim is inferred from this audit.

Two Matlas searches were made: one for arbitrary-step consecutive-row
interpolation certificates, and one for the conjugate of a maximum of
quadratics with common Hessian. Returned results concerned PL descent,
short-step self-contractedness, concave envelopes, or second-order
derivatives of finite maxima. None supplied the needed theorem at its
present hypotheses, so none is used.

Public background search also located the original paper by Jason
Altschuler and Pablo Parrilo, “Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II:
Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization,”
https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530. Its official text is already saved in
downloads/release_middle_sector/2309.16530.txt. The relevant contrast
is methodological: recursive gluing retains and prices original rows.
No coefficient formula or theorem from that paper is transplanted to
this inflated hierarchy. The report's proofs are the explicit row
identities and the complete scalar quadratic given above.

Operationally rg was unavailable (exit127, “rg: command not found”);
basic text tools were used. No mathematical program, numerical sweep,
arithmetic replay, trajectory/recurrence execution, optimizer, solver,
installation, consult, subagent or formal call was made. The written
formulas and inequalities are symbolic text derivations only.

## 9. Exact scope and cap1 handoff

Mechanism1 has a full value-balanced formula with actual internal
transport and an exact whole-polynomial failure. The larger excluded
support class is all backward prefix rows plus outgoing optimum rows,
with terminal mass concentrated on the last queried anchor. Its
discriminator violates Q_(star,J) and Q_(0,J); it is formal only.

Mechanism2 leaves the frozen last-leaf terminal path and keeps all
prefix anchors in a specified occupation average. Its scalar mixing
coefficient is solved exactly. Its target pointwise inequality is
false on a static one-dimensional quadratic with true minimum0 and
radius1, at every allowed m. That function's actual payoff is below
the target. A full-simplex comparison on the same prefix explicitly
prices the anchor information lost by the average.

The two failures do not show that every internal flow or every
geometry-dependent terminal mixture fails. A future transport must
leave the backward-only support class or change the terminal support;
a future mixture must retain enough anchor geometry to survive the
sign-reversing quadratic, with a full-dimensional pointwise proof.
Neither requirement is a theorem-level solution by itself.

Accepted931 gives B_n/D<47/50 for this SAME hierarchy and would turn
a verified full W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) into a negative minimax resolution.
That full W hypothesis remains unproved. The benchmark ratio is an
UPPER comparison and has not been reversed.

Original target: OPEN. Full-class threshold: OPEN.
New verified facts: NONE. New mathematical mechanisms: TWO.
This analytical allocation is exhausted at cap1. No automatic renewal,
live TASK overwrite, finalization, revocation, shutdown, outward
publication, or partial-result completion is performed.

Analytical proof-text example, no forward case run: in dimension one use A=0, alpha=1, derivative phi'(s)=max(-1,min(s,4)), and x=4. This is one static convex 1-smooth function with unique minimizer0 and total core Hessian1. For the original enclosing-m parameters m>=6, a=(m-1)/((m+1)(1+sqrt2))>=5/(7(1+sqrt2))>1/4 (the last inequality follows from sqrt2<10/7<13/7). The first step of every nonempty H_k is 1+a, so from x=4 the actual next point is -4a<-1, outside the negative core. Thus the implication 'all-core entry => all-core suffix' is false. The precise sufficient condition under total core Hessian I is x and -a*x both in the core intersection. For any prefix length t of H_k, the exact core multiplier is (-1)^t beta^t c^{-s_2(t)}. All positive multipliers lie in[0,1] and every negative multiplier in[-a,0], so convexity of the core intersection proves that strengthened condition suffices. The complete derivation is being included in one joint UNVERIFIED C95 draft; no new fact is asserted.
