<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001039 -->
# Positive-height cone simplices: two whole-word obstructions

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit is authorized. Original target OPEN.**

Worker max2, assigned invocation72 after ended baseline71, controller cap1,
ended ceiling72 / attempted73. This is the complete joint mathematical draft
for exactly TWO declared laws. Its lemmas are not accepted facts and may not
be reused as accepted premises. No mathematical program, numerical or
arithmetic replay, executed trajectory, solver, formal call, or subagent was
used. The analysis is symbolic. No automatic renewal is authorized.

## 1. Scope, verdict, and the fixed physical word

The two laws do not produce a stronger lower witness. The proposed uniform
obstructions are:

* Law I, acute two-level heights with a full anisotropic Walsh dictionary:
  the supremum of the actual closest-radius terminal score is exactly
  1/D_m. The entire declared law, including every cap/core/absorption case,
  is covered.
* Law II, arbitrary label heights on a regular Walsh dictionary with an
  explicit schedule-selected cap dilation: the score is at most
  beta^(2(2^m-1)) c^(-2m). Face changes, different gradient norms, and strict
  outgoing-optimum slack at the TRUE closest initial minimizer are allowed.
  In fact the ratio to complete K/ray is less than 2^(1-m).

Law II also yields a broader necessary screen: for ANY cone simplex of the
form conv(0,v_s), a trajectory with zero normal height at every QUERIED point
has the same product bound, irrespective of dimension, cone geometry, or
outgoing-optimum slack. A useful witness must have positive normal height
at a queried point. This is not a bound on arbitrary cap-active trajectories.

Fix any integer m>=1536 ONCE and put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Every child uses these same enclosing parameters. Write h=H_m,
n=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D_m=1+2S, and P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t.
All physical steps, including the actual last leaf 1+a, remain present.
Every h_i>1. We use smoothness1; replacing F by L F gives the original GD
steps h_i/L with exactly the same points and normalized score.

For a constructed objective F with complete minimum set Z and initial point
X_0 outside Z, the score in this report is

    E=2 F(X_n)/dist(X_0,Z)^2.

It is an attained lower witness for the full W_n(h), not an upper bound on W.
The complete K_n(h) of accepted b98bb807d514e80d includes 1/D_m and ALL
single cuts. The accepted statement of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives
K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n. We use only those inclusions. We do not evaluate or
identify the full ray-channel optimum. Accepted 931b9653ce0f7201 gives
B_n/D_m<47/50 at exactly this scope. Its hypothetical full-function upper
bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) remains unproved.

The original all-n/all-h minimax problem, every integer benchmark split,
attained minima, arbitrary finite dimensions, and W=2R are unchanged.
No original-target affirmative or negative conclusion follows from this draft.

## 2. Literal finite-field realization and one static smooth objective

Let r>=2 be finite, N=2^r, and let labels s,t range over F_2^r. For each
nonzero t choose a real b_t>0. Set chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s), where the dot
product in the exponent is in F_2, and define ordinary REAL vectors

    z_s=(b_t chi_t(s))_(t!=0) in R^(N-1),
    v_s=(z_s,k_s) in R^N,
    D=conv(0,{v_s:s in F_2^r}),
    K=cone{v_s:s in F_2^r}.

The heights satisfy k_s>=0 and sum_s k_s>0. In the task's notation,
k_s=eta_s/R with eta_s=R k_s; R>0 is the initial vertical magnitude.
Every nonzero character, including dependent characters, is present. No
expected Gram or change of Euclidean metric is being used. The exact Gram is

    <v_s,v_u>=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2 chi_t(s)chi_t(u)+k_s k_u.       (2.1)

For completeness, sum_s chi_t(s)=0 when t!=0: translate the sum by a label
on which chi_t=-1. Also sum_s chi_t(s)chi_u(s)=N if t=u and0 otherwise,
by applying the same argument to t+u. These give the Fourier identities below.

Put mu=N^(-1)sum_s k_s>0 and khat_t=N^(-1)sum_s k_s chi_t(s). If
g=(w,y)=sum_s alpha_s v_s and tau=sum_s alpha_s, then

    alpha_s=[tau+sum_(t!=0)chi_t(s)w_t/b_t]/N,
    tau=[y-sum_(t!=0)khat_t w_t/b_t]/mu = <ell,g>,
    ell=((-khat_t/(mu b_t))_(t!=0),1/mu).                  (2.2)

Indeed the horizontal equations determine all nonconstant Fourier
coefficients of alpha; the vertical equation determines its constant
coefficient. Thus the N columns v_s are linearly independent. The cone K is
closed, being the image of the closed nonnegative orthant under an
invertible real linear map, and

    D={g in K:<ell,g><=1}.                                (2.3)

The point0 is an actual vertex, and D is a full-dimensional N-simplex.

Use the SINGLE static objective

    F_D(X)=max_(g in D){<X,g>-||g||^2/2}.                  (2.4)

Completing the square makes its unique maximizer G(X)=P_D(X). Here and
below P denotes ordinary Euclidean metric projection. Existence follows
from compactness of D, and strict convexity of squared distance gives
uniqueness. The projection normal condition is

    g in D, <X-g,-g><=0,
    <X-g,v_s-g><=0 for EVERY original label s.             (2.5)

Necessity follows by taking a one-sided derivative along each segment in D.
Sufficiency follows by expanding ||X-u||^2-||X-g||^2 for u in D. It is
enough to check0 and the listed vertices because the expression is affine
in u. Consequently (2.5) includes every remote label and every tie.

If g=P_D(X) and q=P_D(Y), adding their two normal inequalities gives

    ||g-q||^2<=<g-q,X-Y>, hence ||g-q||<=||X-Y||.

Maximization in (2.4) bounds its first-order remainder between0 and
<G(Y)-G(X),Y-X>. This is at most ||Y-X||^2 and is o(||Y-X||).
Therefore F_D is differentiable, convex, and grad F_D=P_D is 1-Lipschitz.
There are interior points of D at which P_D is locally the identity, so
the least global Lipschitz constant is1. No rescaled gradient dynamics occur.

The COMPLETE minimum is

    min F_D=0,
    Z=K^polar={X:<X,v_s><=0 for every s}.                  (2.6)

The forward implication is as follows. If some <X,v_s> is positive,
then a sufficiently small positive multiple theta v_s in D gives a positive
value in (2.4). If every listed inner product is nonpositive, every g in D
has nonpositive <X,g>, and g=0 attains0. In particular (0,-R) is a true
minimizer. It is not generally the closest one.

## 3. All physical rows, drift, and real Grams

At each actual original point X_i define

    g_i=P_D(X_i), r_i=X_i-g_i,
    F_i=<X_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,
    delta_i=<r_i,g_i>>=0,
    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i.                                 (3.1)

The terminal point X_n and g_n are included. If g_i=sum_s alpha_(i,s)v_s,
then alpha_(i,s)>=0, sum_s alpha_(i,s)<=1, and the actual vertical coordinate is

    Y_i=-R-sum_(t<i)h_t sum_s alpha_(t,s) k_s <= -R.        (3.2)

It is never frozen or deleted. For the scaled dictionary of Law II the
same formula uses its actual heights M k_s and its actual coefficients.

All ordered smooth-convex interpolation rows reduce exactly to

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                               (3.3)

The inequality is (2.5) for the real point g_i in D. If X_star is ANY
point of the complete set Z, set g_star=F_star=0. Both optimum orientations
are retained:

    Q_(i,star)=<X_star,g_i><=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0.                               (3.4)

Strict outgoing-optimum slack means -<X_star,g_i>>0. It is distinct from
the normal height delta_i. A cone trajectory can have the former positive
and the latter zero. This distinction is essential here.

Let V be the matrix of real columns v_s and Gamma=V^T V from (2.1). Then
every gradient Gram entry is alpha_i^T Gamma alpha_j. For any chosen
initial displacement Z_0=X_0-X_star, entries involving it are
<Z_0,g_i>=Z_0^T V alpha_i and <Z_0,Z_0>=||Z_0||^2. Coordinates of every
physical X_i follow from X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t V alpha_t. This is the full
literal real Gram; positive semidefiniteness follows by writing any
quadratic form as a squared Euclidean norm. No restricted formal gradient
space, remote-row deletion, or terminal-jet substitution is involved.

## 4. Law I: unequal two-level heights and an acute quotient

Choose any finite r>=2, every b_t>0 arbitrarily, one nonzero character u,
R>0, two heights p,q>0 with p!=q, and any real X. Write b=b_u. Declare

    k_s=p if chi_u(s)=+1, k_s=q if chi_u(s)=-1,
    pq>=b^2,
    X_0=(X e_u,-R).                                      (4.1)

Here e_u is the physical unit coordinate at character u. All these data
are fixed before the run. This entire domain, not only a selected start,
is Law I. Rank may equal m; all other weights may be large, unequal, or
depend on the whole word. A fully specified representative is r=m,
b_t=1+j(t)/N in binary label order, u the first basis label, p=2b,
q=b, R=1, with X specified in the attainment formula below.

For each a in the subgroup u dot a=0, the real orthogonal map that
multiplies horizontal coordinate t by chi_t(a) and fixes the vertical
coordinate permutes the vertices by s -> s+a and preserves D. Its common
fixed subspace consists exactly of the coordinate u and the vertical
coordinate: the only characters trivial on that subgroup are0 and u.
Projection uniqueness forces every projected gradient of a point in that
plane to stay there. The complete trajectory therefore stays there under
ordinary GD, without any coordinate rescaling.

The fixed-plane section of D is exactly the triangle

    T=conv(0,w_+,w_-), w_+=(b,p), w_-=(-b,q).              (4.2)

To see equality, averaging each vertex over the subgroup gives the
corresponding w_sign, so T is contained in the section. Conversely, the
horizontal/vertical components of any convex combination, when projected
to that plane, belong to T. The same symmetry argument applies to the
closest projection onto the full polar Z: group averaging of a minimizer
cannot increase distance from the invariant initial point, and uniqueness
puts the closest point in the fixed plane.

At (X,-R), the two vertex scores are bX-pR and -bX-qR. They cannot both
be positive. If both are nonpositive the run is already at a minimizer;
we omit that zero-radius case from normalized scores. Otherwise let w be
the uniquely positive one, w' the other, l=||w||, e=w/l, and

    t_0=<X_0,e>>0, X_star=X_0-t_0 e.                     (4.3)

This is the TRUE closest initial minimizer. For example if w=w_+, then

    X_star=[(pX+bR)/(b^2+p^2)](p,-b),
    <X_star,w_->=-b(p+q)(pX+bR)/(b^2+p^2)<0,
    <X_star,w_+>=0.                                     (4.4)

Here bX>pR implies X>0. Thus X_star lies in Z and its residual t_0e is
in K and perpendicular to X_star. For any Y in Z,
<t_0e,Y-X_star><=0; expanding squared distance proves closestness. The
negative sign case follows by replacing X by -X and exchanging p,q.
The exact radius is t_0, whereas ||X_0-(0,-R)||=|X| is only another
minimizer's distance. We do not substitute the latter in the upper bound.

The acute condition is <w,w'>=pq-b^2>=0. Whenever the inactive score is
nonpositive, set t=<X_i,e>. The exact projection is

    g_i=clip(t,[0,l]) e.                                 (4.5)

Here is a direct guard check, not merely a symmetry assertion. For
0<t<l the residual is perpendicular to e; its inner product with w'
is <X_i,w'>-t<e,w'><=0. For t>=l, its inner product with g_i is
l(t-l)>=0, its guard against w is equality, and its inner product with
w' is nonpositive. The guard against w'-g_i is therefore nonpositive.
For t<=0 both vertex scores are nonpositive and g_i=0. These prove (2.5)
on0,w,w'. Every original remote vertex has, against a fixed-plane
residual, the same inner product as the corresponding w_sign. Thus every
original normal inequality holds. Subtracting h_i g_i cannot increase
the inactive score because h_i>=0 and <w,w'>>=0. Induction proves (4.5)
for the WHOLE trajectory, including a crossing into the polar minimum.

The exact scalar dynamics and physical points are

    t_(i+1)=t_i-h_i clip(t_i,[0,l]),
    X_i=X_star+t_i e.                                   (4.6)

This identity pays the vertical drift e_vertical times every scalar update.
It also makes the full Gram explicit: <g_i,g_j>=z_i z_j and
<X_0-X_star,g_i>=t_0 z_i, where z_i=clip(t_i,[0,l]).

One can write every query without an implicit optimization. Let k be the
first i<n with t_0-lP_i<=l, or k=n if none exists. For 0<=i<=k,
t_i=t_0-lP_i. If k<n and t_k<=0, set t_i=t_k for all i>=k. If k<n and
0<t_k<=l, set t_i=(1-h_k)t_k<=0 for every i>=k+1. These are all cases,
since the earlier gradients equal l and a nonpositive state is absorbing.
The true minimum set remains the full polar cone, not this one ray.

## 5. Law I: exact whole-word terminal and sharp obstruction

Since every h_i>1, a queried t_i in (0,l] is sent to a nonpositive
state and never recovers. Consequently a positive terminal is possible
only if all queried gradients equal le. It occurs exactly when
t_0>lS, and then t_n=t_0-lS>0. To prove the converse direction, if
t_0>lS then every queried state in the cap formula is
t_0-lP_i>l(S-P_i)>=l h_i>l, so that formula is indeed valid through
the actual final update. If t_0<=lS, either absorption occurs earlier or
the all-cap formula gives a nonpositive terminal. Thus no case is omitted.

For z=t_0/l>0, define the one-sided unit Huber function

    Hplus(v)=0 for v<=0;
             v^2/2 for 0<=v<=1;
             v-1/2 for v>=1.

Equation (2.4), or the direct cap projection, gives the exact score

    E=2 Hplus(z-S)/z^2.                                 (5.1)

This is an exact formula for the static body's ACTUAL terminal value,
even when the absorbed physical point differs from X_star+(t_0-lS)e.
For z<=S it is0. For S<z<S+1 it is (z-S)^2/z^2, increasing in z and
at most 1/(S+1)^2<=1/(2S+1). For z>=S+1 it is
(2z-D_m)/z^2, and

    z^2-D_m(2z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2>=0.

Therefore E<=1/D_m for every parameter choice in Law I. Equality is
attained at z=D_m: choose the positive side and select explicitly

    X=[(b^2+p^2)D_m+pR]/b.                              (5.2)

Then t_0=lD_m and X_n has positive Huber argument S+1. No supremum-only
limiting instance or incomplete minimum set is involved. Thus

    sup_(Law I) E=1/D_m<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),
    B_n E<47/50.                                        (5.3)

Positive heights relative to the nearby minimizer (0,-R) do not help this
law: at the closest minimizer X_star, <X_star,g_i>=0 for every nonzero
gradient. This conclusion was derived, not imposed as a frozen carrier.

## 6. Cone geometry, independently derived for Law II

For a closed convex cone K let g=P_K(X). Taking competitors0 and2g in
the normal condition gives <X-g,g>=0; taking arbitrary cone points gives
r=X-g in K^polar. Conversely g in K, r in K^polar, and <r,g>=0 prove
the projection normal inequalities. Moreover r is the closest point in
K^polar: for z in K^polar, <g,z-r><=0, and squared-distance expansion
proves ||X-r||<=||X-z||. Consequently

    dist(X,K^polar)=||P_K(X)||,
    max_(g in K){<X,g>-||g||^2/2}=||P_K(X)||^2/2.          (6.1)

This is a complete direct proof of the cone decomposition used here;
no unverified epigraph/universality finding is a premise.

Projection existence onto the closed cone follows by restricting a
distance-minimizing sequence to a bounded ball and taking a convergent
subsequence; strict convexity gives uniqueness. Thus closed unbounded
cones cause no attainment issue in this argument.

For h>=1, a conic gradient step is X'=r-(h-1)g. Since r is a genuine
minimizer in K^polar,

    dist(X',K^polar)<=(h-1)||g||.                        (6.2)

This inequality is Euclidean and dimension-independent. It does not require
two rays, fixed support, commutative face projectors, or equal gradient norms.

There is also a diagnostic equivalence for the FINITE simplex D. If
g=P_D(X) and delta=<X-g,g>, then

    delta=0  iff  g=P_K(X) belongs to D.                 (6.3)

Indeed delta=0 and the vertex guards imply <X-g,v_s><=0 for every s,
while the residual is perpendicular to g. Equation (6.1) identifies the
cone projection. Conversely a cone projection in D remains the closest
point in D and has zero delta. If delta>0, writing g=sum alpha_s v_s
and using <X-g,v_s><=delta yields
delta<=delta sum alpha_s<=delta. Hence sum alpha_s=1. It is the finite
cap, with positive normal multiplier, that distinguishes this case from
conic motion. Merely lying on the cap with zero multiplier is insufficient.

## 7. Law II: arbitrary multilevel heights and an explicit paid start

Choose any finite r>=2, a scalar b>0, R>0, all label heights k_s>=0
with positive total, and a coefficient A>R k_0/[b^2(N-1)]. There is no
two-level, common-height, or small-height restriction. Use base vectors

    z_s^0=(b chi_t(s))_(t!=0), u_s=(z_s^0,k_s),
    B=b^2(N-1), L0=sqrt(B+k_0^2),
    X_0=(A z_0^0,-R),
    t0=(AB-Rk_0)/(B+k_0^2)>0,
    r0=(AB-Rk_0)/L0>0,
    gamma0=(R+A k_0)/(B+k_0^2)>0,
    X_star=(k_0 gamma0 z_0^0,-B gamma0).                 (7.1)

The symbol B here is a local squared vertex norm and is NOT benchmark B_n.
The regular Walsh Gram is

    Gamma^0_su=<u_s,u_u>=b^2(N 1_(s=u)-1)+k_s k_u.       (7.2)

We have X_0-X_star=t0 u_0, <X_star,u_0>=0, and for EVERY s!=0,

    <X_star,u_s>=-gamma0(b^2 k_0+B k_s)<=0.              (7.3)

Thus X_star is the closest point of the FULL cone polar by (6.1), and
the exact initial distance is r0. Formula (7.3) is an all-label guard,
including zero heights and ties. It does not replace the full minimum by
the origin. For comparison, the nearby minimizer (0,-R) has distance
A sqrt(B), generally larger than r0.

Let ell0 be the vector (2.2) for these base columns u_s. Define the literal,
unevaluated schedule quantity

    Q=product_(i<n) max(1,h_i-1)
     =product_(j=0)^(m-1) max(1,d_j)^(2^(m-1-j)),
    M=1+2 ||ell0|| r0 Q.                                (7.4)

This is an explicit finite formula from the known word and selected
parameters, not an optimizer, trajectory-dependent scale, numerical replay,
or after-the-fact change of dictionary. Now freeze the actual body

    v_s=M u_s,
    D=conv(0,{M u_s}), K=cone{u_s},
    actual b_t=M b, actual eta_s=R M k_s.                (7.5)

The actual Gram is M^2 Gamma^0, the true polar is unchanged, and the
static smooth function is (2.4) for this scaled body. Scaling ALL columns
selects the body size; it does not scale a coordinate of the GD metric.

A completely specified schedule-only member, if one wants no free
parameter selection, is r=m, b=R=1, k_s=(1+j(s))/D_m with j(s) the
binary integer label in {0,...,N-1}, and
A=(R k_0+1)/B. Then AB-Rk_0=1, all N heights are distinct and positive,
and (7.4) fixes M. This example is included in the obstruction; no gain
is claimed for it. All members of the much broader parameter domain above
are covered uniformly.

## 8. Law II: every face, physical query, and cap guard

Let pi_i=product_(t<i)(h_t-1), with pi_0=1. Then 0<pi_i<=Q for every
0<=i<=n. We prove simultaneously by induction that the actual finite-body
gradient is the cone projection and

    ||g_i||<=r0 pi_i,
    <ell0,g_i>/M<1/2.                                   (8.1)

At i=0 the base cone projection is t0 u_0 and has norm r0. By
Cauchy--Schwarz its base coefficient sum is
<ell0,g_0><=||ell0||r0<M/2, so g_0 is in the actual simplex and its
projection normal inequalities hold against every M u_s and0. Suppose
the statement is known at a queried i. Equation (6.2) bounds the distance
of X_(i+1) to the full polar by (h_i-1)||g_i||<=r0 pi_(i+1).
The cone projection at X_(i+1) therefore has norm at most this amount.
Its base coefficient sum is nonnegative and at most
||ell0||r0 Q<M/2. It belongs to D and (6.3) identifies it with the
actual finite-body gradient. This proves (8.1) through the terminal n.

All cone faces are allowed; no itinerary was assumed in this induction.
For an exact piecewise formula for every physical query, let U_J denote
the matrix of base columns u_s for a subset J of labels. Every nonempty
principal Gram Gamma^0_J is positive definite, because the complete
matrix of columns is invertible. Define

    alpha_J(X)=(Gamma^0_J)^(-1) U_J^T X,
    P_J=U_J (Gamma^0_J)^(-1) U_J^T.                       (8.2)

The region for positive support J is exactly

    alpha_J(X)>0 componentwise,
    <X-P_J X,u_s><=0 for EVERY s outside J.              (8.3)

On that region g=P_J X and its actual simplex coefficients are
alpha_s/M on J,0 off J, and the coefficient of the actual vertex0 is
1-sum alpha_s/M>1/2. For J empty all listed vertex scores are
nonpositive and g=0. Boundary zeros are removed from J, so the positive
support is unique; inactive normal equalities are allowed. These formulas
follow from the normal condition, not from an expected face or a projected
transcript supplied by another construction.

Writing J_i for the uniquely specified region of X_i, every original
point and query is exactly

    X_i=(I-h_(i-1)P_(J_(i-1))) ... (I-h_0 P_(J_0)) X_0,
    g_i=P_(J_i)X_i, 0<=i<=n,                            (8.4)

with the empty product at i=0. The order of these generally noncommuting
matrices is the physical order of H_m. Equations (8.2)--(8.4) are finite
piecewise formulas, with ALL remote guards; no such matrices or trajectories
were evaluated by a program. They serve to identify the actual queries,
not to substitute a recurrence for the terminal estimate: the independent
uniform terminal bound is proved in Section9.

Using base cone coefficients alpha_i (extended by zeros), the full
real Gram and actual vertical evolution specialize to

    <g_i,g_j>=alpha_i^T Gamma^0 alpha_j,
    <X_0-X_star,g_i>=t0 e_0^T Gamma^0 alpha_i,
    ||X_0-X_star||^2=r0^2,
    Y_i=-R-sum_(t<i)h_t sum_s alpha_(t,s) k_s.            (8.5)

Here e_0 in the Gram formula is the unit vector at label0 in the
coefficient space R^N, not a new physical coordinate or a changed metric.

Every coordinate, common component, cap guard, optimum row, and terminal
row is covered. The exact terminal value is F_D(X_n)=||g_n||^2/2,
and the exact score is alpha_n^T Gamma^0 alpha_n/r0^2. This is a real
body with an explicit initialization, not a representation theorem for a
preexisting adverse transcript.

## 9. Law II: whole-word quantitative obstruction

Equation (8.1) and the cone value give

    E<=pi_n^2.                                          (9.1)

The literal product over H_j is exactly

    product_(h in H_j)(h-1)=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).           (9.2)

At j=0 both sides are1. At the parent the product is d_j times the
square of the child product; substituting d_j=beta c^(j-1) gives the
stated expression with j+1. This symbolic induction retains the enclosing
m and every actual final leaf. It is not an evaluated recurrence.
At the physical horizon n=2^m-1,

    E<=beta^(2n)c^(-2m).                                (9.3)

For clarity the elementary mass estimate needed for comparison can also
be checked jointly. Put v=a/(c-2). Since c>lambda>2,
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1 and0<beta<1, so0<v<1. Substitution into
A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, A_0=1, gives

    A_j=1+sum H_j=(1-v)2^j+v c^j.

Hence A_m<c^m and D_m=2A_m-1<2c^m. Since complete K and complete
F_ray each contain1/D_m, equations (9.1)--(9.3) prove

    E/K_n(h)<=D_m E<2 beta^(2n)c^(-m)<2^(1-m),
    E/F_ray_n(h)<2^(1-m),
    B_n E<(47/25) beta^(2n)c^(-m).                       (9.4)

These are uniform over EVERY declared rank, height list, start parameter,
and geometry in Law II. They give a vanishing ratio to the existing
complete channel, not merely an inability to prove a nonvanishing gain.

There is a useful extension of the same argument that does not assert a
third construction law. For ANY simplex D=conv(0,v_s) with closed
finitely generated cone K, suppose delta_i=0 at every queried i<n.
By (6.3) every actual update is conic, so repeated (6.2) gives
dist(X_n,K^polar)<=pi_n dist(X_0,K^polar). At the terminal we only need

    F_D(X_n)<=max_(g in K){<X_n,g>-||g||^2/2}
             =dist(X_n,K^polar)^2/2.

Thus (9.3) holds even without assuming the terminal cap is inactive.
This extension permits arbitrary anisotropy, arbitrary initial points,
and arbitrary positive outgoing-optimum slack. It proves that any
cone-simplex witness exceeding the product bound must have delta_i>0
at some QUERIED original point, not merely a selected terminal jet.
Nothing here bounds the contribution of a positive delta_i.

## 10. A genuine changing-face member with strict closest-optimum slack

This is an explicit member of Law II, used to test the scope of its
obstruction. It is not a third law and is not advertised as a useful
whole-word witness. It refutes the shortcuts that Law II freezes its
direction or silently makes all outgoing optimum rows tight.

Take r=2,N=4,b=R=A=1. Label the four vertices0,1,2,3 and put

    delta=a/100,
    (k_0,k_1,k_2,k_3)=(delta,2delta,3delta,4delta).

All dependent character weights equal1 and all four heights differ.
One has0<a<1/2 because a<1/lambda<1/2, so0<delta<1/200.
Use the actual cap scale M prescribed in (7.4), with these parameters.

The initial cone gradient and the first ACTUAL leaf update are

    theta=(3-delta)/(3+delta^2)>0,
    g_0=theta u_0,
    h_0=1+a,
    X_1=X_0-(1+a)theta u_0.

For J={1,2,3}, put k=(2,3,4)^T delta and define

    T=(1+a)theta-1,
    U=1+(1+a)theta delta,
    v=(11,12,13)^T delta/4,
    C=55 delta^2/2.

The vector of scores U_J^T X_1 is T 1-U k. The exact face Gram is

    Gamma_J=4I-11^T+kk^T.

The inverse of 4I-11^T is (I+11^T)/4 (multiply the matrices to check),
so its product with k is v and k^T v=C. Substitution into Gamma_J alpha
=T1-U k yields the explicit solution

    alpha=T 1-v (U+9delta T)/(1+C).                      (10.1)

This identity can be checked by multiplying the displayed matrices;
no numerical system solve or unverified inverse formula is required.
We now check the signs analytically. Since

    T=a-(1+a)delta(1+delta)/(3+delta^2),

one has a/2<T<a. Also0<U<2 and U+9delta T<3, using a<1/2 and
delta<1/200. Each component subtracted in (10.1) is less than
39delta/4=39a/400<a/4. Thus every alpha_s>a/4>0.

Let g_1=U_J alpha and r_1=X_1-g_1. The three J-normal equations are
r_1 dot u_s=0. Its vertical coordinate is strictly negative, because
X_1 has vertical coordinate -1-(1+a)theta delta<0 and the vertical
coordinate of U_J alpha is positive. The sum of ALL four base columns is
(0,10delta), hence

    <r_1,u_0>=10delta (r_1)_vertical<0.                  (10.2)

This is the sole remaining remote cone guard; together with alpha>0
it proves g_1 is the exact projection. The already proved whole-word
scale guard gives coefficient sum/M<1/2 for both g_0 and g_1. Thus g_0
is in the interior of the edge from0 to M u_0, whereas g_1 is in the
relative interior of the face conv(0,M u_1,M u_2,M u_3). Neither query
is a vertex. Their directions differ because the four base columns are
linearly independent. Their norms differ since0<||g_1||<=a||g_0||<||g_0||.

The closest initial minimizer of (7.1) has

    <X_star,u_j>=-gamma0 delta(1+3(j+1))<0, j=1,2,3.

Consequently <X_star,g_1><0. The outgoing optimum row is strictly slack
about the TRUE closest minimum, not only about the more distant (0,-1).
The vertical coordinate moved during the first ordinary step. Nevertheless
the actual ENTIRE trajectory still satisfies (9.3)--(9.4).

This example shows why strict outgoing-optimum slack plus a legal local
noncommuting or changing-face transition is not itself a terminal gain.

## 11. Exact surviving interface and scope audit

Law I closes a full acute, two-level, symmetry-invariant domain. Its
assumptions do not cover an obtuse quotient, three or more independent
height levels with general starts, or general noninvariant initialization.
The scalar collapse was proved from the symmetry and acute normal guards.
It is not a universal theorem about all positive-height cone simplices.

Law II closes an explicitly selected cone regime. Its multilevel heights,
full dimension, changing faces, and positive closest-optimum slack survive;
what fails is terminal performance while the positive-multiplier cap is
never used. The general zero-height screen explains that failure without
an assumed face itinerary. The sufficient cap dilation is not claimed
necessary or optimal. Reducing M to make the finite cap active is outside
the declared law, not an unproved extension of its bound.

The still-live geometric interface is therefore precise: select from the
known H_m one finite cone simplex and an initial horizontal state whose
trajectory uses positive cap multipliers delta_i at physical queried
indices, while genuinely changing conic faces/gradient norms and paying
the whole closest-radius cost and the moving vertical coordinate. At least
one such multiplier is necessary to beat (9.3); this draft does NOT prove
that one is sufficient, or that recurrent returns are necessary. An
obtuse two-level quotient with active caps, or a noninvariant multilevel
dictionary with active caps, remains outside both obstructions. The
unresolved step is a quantitative entire-word terminal/radius estimate
for such an explicitly selected law, including all remote normals and
the actual last leaf. No new third law is opened in this capped invocation.

No claim is made that all such remaining regimes are impossible. No
restricted-family upper bound is promoted to the full W. No exact original
B_n lower comparison is claimed. The universal epigraph and sparse/simplex
awareness records e967ef4e5752cf2f, d12c90331a4d3e5d, and
5178ca3dbb6aece4 are not used as theorem premises. The previous thin-mixed
or all-vertex law dc755a6e62f17395 was not retuned.

## 12. Retrieval and operational record

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving, and
construct-counterexamples. Active program: positive-height cone-simplex
geometry; stage: mature_subproblem; search mode: mutation; missing mechanism:
paid entire-word changing faces with a useful actual terminal value.

Matlas returned arXiv2006.08426 Theorem1 (cone decomposition),
2107.07257 LemmaS17 (polyhedral projection faces), and2302.01517 Corollary3.2
(pseudonorm duality). The third was discarded; the first two supply
background vocabulary, not downstream theorem premises.

Exact paper downloaded: Hassan Mortagy, Swati Gupta, Sebastian Pokutta,
Walking in the Shadow: A New Perspective on Descent Directions for
Constrained Minimization, arXiv2006.08426. Browser-extracted text around
Theorem1 and the Section3 normal-inequality proofs was read. Theorem1 uses
the negative polar convention employed here; its constrained-descent
algorithm is not the unconstrained GD objective of this report. The paper
cites the cone decomposition rather than proving it at that location;
the search for a local proof returned "No matching text found". All needed
projection and decomposition identities were instead proved in Sections2
and6. The exact background PDF arXiv1810.04206 was also downloaded and its
polar/projection arguments inspected; no theorem from it is a premise.
There are NO external proof dependencies in this joint draft.

Latest shared guidance 5fee1ea5f63fd8fc was read; it explicitly retains
max2r72 under the existing cap and zero formal/numerical allowance. The
new native simplex advisory was read only as awareness; no portion is a

# Original-radius gradient interface: rigorous partial derivation and surviving class

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. The frozen full gradient claim is NOT proved or refuted.**

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926, worker max3, invocation88 after ended87;
completed ceiling88 / attempted89, controller cap1. Applicable guidance
c599f239a314491b; plan4593a1342e03ac83. This is the bounded analytical
invocation specified by TASK.md. No verifier call or new fact is claimed.

The assignment's decisive first result was not reached. Mechanism2 was
therefore NOT activated. In particular this report does not assemble the
whole two-cap objective theorem, and does not replace the frozen gradient
claim by a different global invariant. It records the actual extent of the
first mechanism, including an all-position two-crossing original-radius
argument and a quantitative multiple-crossing reduction.

The earlier complete reports37660b4102b12fe7 and b726f6dc7baa6b7b were read
for awareness. Neither is an accepted premise. Every mathematical claim
made by the new partial derivation below is derived here. The prior
balanced objective candidate and smaller-tail objective payment retain
their previous JOINT UNVERIFIED status and exact scopes. The fact graph
has not changed. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is used only for its scope: its
negative-resolution implication requires a bound on the FULL finite-
dimensional W supremum, not a scalar-family theorem.

## 1. Frozen object and precise partial result

Keep ONE enclosing integer m>=1536 throughout, including every child:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, b=c-2,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D=1+2S_m.

The bridge indices are 0<=j<m. The auxiliary d_m is not an added physical
step. H_j has exactly 2^j-1 chronological updates. Fix the same function
throughout every actual trajectory:

    0<kappa<=1, v=1-kappa, B_+>=kappa, B_->=kappa,
    K=min(B_+,B_-), B=max(B_+,B_-),
    u_sigma=v+B_sigma,
    G_sigma(r)=min(B_sigma,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(sigma*r)=sigma*G_sigma(r), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.

Here sigma is either +1 or -1. Designate a smaller-cap ray sigma_S with
cap K and the opposite ray sigma_L with cap B. When the caps tie, either
fixed designation is permitted. All symbols in this report are LOCAL;
in particular H_j, a, b, A_j and R do not redefine unrelated glossary
symbols or the project's normalized functional mathcal R_n.

For each 1<=j<=m, take an original signed start s, set R=|s|, and run
ALL entries of the actual H_j by x_next=x-h*g(x). Its output is Psi_j(s)
and its terminal gradient magnitude is p_n=|g(Psi_j(s))|. A strict
crossing means two consecutive nonzero iterates with opposite signs.
Zero is absorbing. Every denominator R below is this original start's
true distance to the unique minimizer zero.

The assignment's frozen claim is

    p_n <= beta*R/d_j.                               (G)

The new JOINT UNVERIFIED partial conclusion is:

1. If a history has at most TWO strict crossings, then, for R>0,

       p_n < R/(2*d_j).                              (1.1)

   This holds at arbitrary crossing positions and levels, on both rays,
   for every allowed pair of caps and every kappa. It includes tail
   inputs, tail terminals, core and middle transitions, and zeros.

2. Define the explicit coefficient

       gamma=c*b^2 <153/343<1/2.

   If a history has at least TWO strict crossings, then

       p_n <gamma*K.                                (1.2)

   If any crossed history terminates on the designated larger ray,
   there is the stronger p_n<b*K. All such terminals are strictly
   before their actual cap; (1.2) also puts a multiply-crossed smaller
   terminal strictly before its cap.

3. Consequently (G) also holds for every multiply-crossed history with

       R>=gamma*d_j*K/beta,                          (1.3)

   and the sufficient threshold is R>=b*d_j*K/beta for a larger-ray
   terminal. These are conditions on the ACTUAL original radius.

Since beta>999/1000, (1.1) is strictly stronger than (G) on its scope.
The cap bounds (1.2)-(1.3) are not an unrestricted original-radius
estimate: their remaining small-R regime is stated in Section9.

There is no claimed counterexample to (G). Section7 gives an actual
complete-word counterexample ONLY to extending the balanced a-grid.
Section8 proves that an unconstrained product of crossing gains is too
large as a relaxation, and explicitly does NOT realize it as a trajectory.

## 2. Legality, energies, and elementary payments

For each fixed ray the derivative has the three pieces

    kappa*r       for 0<=r<=1,
    r-v           for 1<=r<=v+B_sigma,
    B_sigma       for r>=v+B_sigma.

The middle can have zero length. These formulas match at both knots.
The signed derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and piecewise has
slopes kappa,1,0. Partitioning an interval at its knots shows that g is
1-Lipschitz. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth.
Since G_sigma(r)>0 for r>0, the unique minimizer is0 and f(0)=0.
In particular R=|s| is the original closest-minimum distance. The ray
energies, from integrating the displayed derivative, are

    2f(sigma*r)=kappa*r^2                         (r<=1),
                 (r-v)^2+kappa*v                 (1<=r<=u_sigma),
                 2B_sigma*r-B_sigma^2-v*(2B_sigma-kappa)
                                                  (r>=u_sigma).

No smaller-tail energy is replaced by a pre-cap formula.
For every ray and radius,

    0<=G_sigma(r)<=min(r,B_sigma).                  (2.1)

At a level-k strict crossing, let y be the source radius, p its actual
source gradient magnitude and z the destination radius. Exactly

    z=(1+d_k)*p-y <=d_k*p.                         (2.2)

On any segment with no crossing and a positive terminal, every point
stays on one ray and its radii and gradients decrease. If its start is
Z, its total mass is M, and its final gradient is q, then summing EVERY
step of this segment gives

    Z>=r_end+M*q >=(1+M)*q.                        (2.3)

A zero hit is treated separately because it makes the final gradient
zero. A noncrossing complete H_j satisfies p_n<=R/A_j.

All reasoning below applies to actual updates of this fixed f. Source
and destination gradients are not interchangeable, and no child input,
cap, or curvature is independently selected.

## 3. Quantitative masses and a sharpened physical product

The following elementary bounds hold uniformly:

    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    2/5<a<b<3/7<1/2,
    beta>999/1000, cb>1.

For c, use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/1000; the upper comparison
(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7 gives the displayed bound. The lower bound on a
follows from a>(999/1000)*(7/17)>2/5. The identity
lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda imply cb>1>beta, so a=beta/c<b.
Also b=c-2<3/7. These imply gamma=cb^2<153/343<1/2 and b<gamma.

The literal word masses satisfy

    A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-a/b)*2^j+(a/b)*c^j.                    (3.1)

The last expression satisfies the initial condition and the recurrence
because 2a/b+a=ca/b. Since0<a/b<1, it follows that

    A_j>d_j/b for every j>=0,
    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    d_j/A_j>=a for every j>=0.                    (3.2)

The last inequality is equality at j=0 and strict for j>=1.

Every occurrence of a level-k bridge within H_l has k<l. Its literal
prefix and suffix masses M,N within that H_l obey

    M,N>=S_k,  M+(1+d_k)+N=S_l.

Put X=1+M, Y=1+N. Then

    X,Y>=A_k, X+Y=A_l-d_k,
    XY>=A_k*(A_l-d_k-A_k),                         (3.3)

where the last inequality is (X-A_k)(Y-A_k)>=0.

We strengthen the source's product bound to

    (1+M)*(1+N)>d_l*d_k/gamma.                    (3.4)

To prove it, let n=l-k>=1. Unrolling the finite mass identity
algebraically, without evaluating any recurrence, gives

    A_l=2^n*A_k+d_k*(c^n-2^n)/b.

Consequently, using A_k>d_k/b,

    A_l-d_k-A_k
       =(2^n-1)*A_k+d_k*((c^n-2^n)/b-1)
       >d_l/b-d_k*(1+1/b)
       >=(d_l/b)*(1-(b+1)/c)
       =d_l/(bc).

The penultimate line uses d_k<=d_l/c; the final equality uses b+1=c-1.
Together with A_k>d_k/b and (3.3), this proves (3.4), including k=0.
This is a symbolic inequality for every physical occurrence; no balanced
caps, chosen crossing subset, or symmetry of trajectory masses is used.

One further mass comparison will pay exterior ancestors in Section5.
Let a literal H_(l+1) lie inside H_j, and let E be the total mass outside
that occurrence. Then E=A_j-A_(l+1)>=0, and

    A_l+E > A_j/c.                                (3.5)

Indeed c*(A_l+E)-A_j=(c-1)A_j-c*(A_l+d_l), which is at least
(c-1)*(2A_l+d_l)-c*(A_l+d_l)=b*A_l-d_l>0.
All masses outside the chosen occurrence remain in E; none is discarded.

## 4. Zero or one crossing: original-radius conclusion

Without crossings, (2.3) and (3.2) give

    p_n<=R/A_j <b*R/d_j <R/(2d_j).

Suppose there is exactly one crossing, of level k. Let P,T be its
ENTIRE original prefix and suffix masses, and p its source gradient.
The prefix and suffix are noncrossing. Thus

    (1+P)*p<=R,
    (1+T)*p_n<=z<=d_k*p.

Apply (3.4) with containing word H_j to obtain

    p_n<=d_k*R/((1+P)*(1+T))
        <gamma*R/d_j <R/(2d_j).                    (4.1)

This argument includes both crossing directions, every source region,
every terminal region and every physical crossing level, including the
leaf. Its prefix is the prefix from the original start; it is not a
first-crossing price imported at a reset child.

## 5. Exactly two crossings: all positions, all original ancestors

Assume the positive-terminal history has exactly two crossings, at
levels u and v in chronological order. Let P,M,T be respectively the
full original mass before, between and after these two physical steps,
and put X=1+P,Y=1+M,Z=1+T. If the source gradients are p_1,p_2, then
(2.2)-(2.3) give

    X*p_1<=R, Y*p_2<=d_u*p_1, Z*p_n<=d_v*p_2,
    p_n/R<=d_u*d_v/(XYZ).                         (5.1)

This is a valid upper bound on the ACTUAL history. We do not assume it
is sharp. The tree gives two exhaustive cases.

Choose the smallest literal subtree H_(l+1) containing BOTH crossing
steps. Its center has level l. Either that center is one of the two
crossings, or the two crossings lie in its different children and the
center does not cross. These alternatives also cover equal crossing
levels in disjoint children.

Let E_L,E_R be the physical masses before and after this whole subtree
in the original H_j; E=E_L+E_R=A_j-A_(l+1). These exterior portions have
no crossings because the two selected ones are all the crossings.
They add to X and Z, not to the middle gap Y.

### 5A. One crossing is the center of the smallest subtree

First suppose the second crossing is the center, of level l, and the
first, of level k<l, lies in its left H_l. Within that left H_l let
X_0,Y_0 be one plus the masses before and after the level-k crossing.
Then X_0,Y_0<=A_l, and (3.4) gives

    X_0*Y_0>d_l*d_k/gamma.

In the whole original history,

    X=X_0+E_L, Y=Y_0, Z=A_l+E_R.

Since X_0<=A_l, multiplication of these nonnegative quantities yields

    XYZ >=X_0*Y_0*(A_l+E_L+E_R)
        > (d_l*d_k/gamma)*(A_l+E)
        > d_l*d_k*A_j/(c*gamma).

If instead the first crossing is the center and the second is in its
right H_l, interchange the left and right roles: the same proof uses
X=A_l+E_L, Y=Y_0, Z=Z_0+E_R, with Y_0*Z_0>d_l*d_k/gamma and Z_0<=A_l.
This is a comparison of physical masses; it does not reverse the actual
trajectory or assume equal gradients on the two rays.

Combining with (5.1) and A_j>d_j/b gives

    p_n/R <c*gamma/A_j <c^2*b^3/d_j <1/(2d_j).      (5.2)

For the last comparison use

    c^2*b^3 <(17/7)^2*(3/7)^3=7803/16807<1/2.

### 5B. The center between the two crossings does not cross

Now the first crossing is in the left H_l and the second in the right
H_l. Let X_0,Z_0 be one plus the masses before the first and after the
second, within this smallest subtree. Thus

    A_u<=X_0<=A_l, A_v<=Z_0<=A_l.

The middle gap includes the entire immediate right H_u of the first
crossing, the level-l center, and the entire immediate left H_v of the
second. These three pieces are disjoint physical pieces. Therefore

    Y>=A_u+A_v+d_l>d_l.

The original gaps are X=X_0+E_L and Z=Z_0+E_R. Since X_0,Z_0<=A_l,

    XZ>=X_0*Z_0*(1+E/A_l)
       >(d_u*d_v/b^2)*(1+E/A_l).

Using d_l/A_l>=a and (3.5) then gives

    XYZ >(d_u*d_v/b^2)*(d_l/A_l)*(A_l+E)
        >a*d_u*d_v*A_j/(c*b^2)
        >a*d_u*d_v*d_j/(c*b^3).

Consequently

    p_n/R <c*b^3/(a*d_j)<1/(2d_j),                (5.3)

because

    c*b^3/a <(17/7)*(3/7)^3*(5/2)=2295/4802<1/2.

Cases5A and5B exhaust the positions of two crossings. In particular a
large noncrossing ancestor BETWEEN crossings has been paid in Y, and
ancestors BEFORE or AFTER both crossings have been paid in E_L,E_R.
No root-only itinerary, equal run mass, cap replacement, or independent
choice of a second child's input occurs. Together with Section4 and
absorbing zeros, this proves the scoped conclusion (1.1).

## 6. Arbitrarily many crossings: quantitative last-two capture

For a crossed history ending on the larger ray, choose its last
crossing, at level k. Its source is on the smaller ray, with gradient
at most K, so its landing satisfies z<=d_k*K. Its complete remaining
suffix has no crossings, contains the immediate right H_k, and has
mass T>=S_k. Thus

    p_n<=d_k*K/(1+T)<=d_k*K/A_k<b*K.                (6.1)

This rederives the source capture rather than adopting it as a fact.

Suppose now that there are at least two crossings and the terminal is
on the smaller ray. The penultimate crossing is small-to-large, at
level l; write L for its landing. Then L<=d_l*K. The last crossing is
large-to-small, at level k, with source gradient p. Between these two
consecutive crossings all radii on the larger ray decrease.

If the last crossing is AFTER the whole immediate right H_l of the
penultimate step, that child is noncrossing. Its endpoint gradient is
at most L/A_l<b*K; all later larger-ray gradients up to the last source
are no larger. The final suffix contains H_k and is noncrossing, so

    p_n<=d_k*p/A_k <b*p <b^2*K <gamma*K.            (6.2)

If the last crossing is INSIDE that immediate right H_l, let M,N be
its prefix and suffix masses inside H_l. The consecutive-crossing
property gives

    (1+M)*p<=L<=d_l*K.

The mass T of the whole suffix after the last crossing is at least N.
With z its last landing, (2.2)-(2.3) give

    (1+T)*p_n<=z<=d_k*p.

Therefore (3.4) applied to this actual bridge occurrence yields

    p_n<=d_l*d_k*K/((1+M)*(1+N))<gamma*K.           (6.3)

The case l=0 has an empty immediate child and only the first alternative
can occur. Skipped ancestors and any earlier crossings remain present;
they do not affect the fact that these are the final two crossings.

Since b<gamma, (6.1)-(6.3) prove (1.2) on either terminal ray. They also
show that a smaller-tail terminal cannot have two crossings: there its
gradient would equal K, contradicting p_n<gamma*K<K. This is a
quantitative extension of the earlier last-two-crossing tail exclusion.

Dividing by the ORIGINAL R proves (G) under exactly the thresholds
(1.3), or the smaller threshold using (6.1) on the larger ray. The
inequality p_n<gamma*K alone does not establish (G) below those thresholds.

## 7. Legal counterexample to the unrestricted balanced a-grid, not (G)

The tempting induction extension

    R<=d_j*Bmax implies |g(Psi_j(s))|<=a*Bmax       (7.1)

is false for the present arbitrary-cap family. This does not refute the
assignment's weaker original-radius bound (G).

For ANY enclosing m>=1536 and any 1<=j<=m, take the fixed function

    kappa=1, v=0, B_-=1, B_+=A_j/d_j,
    original signed start s=-A_j.

Here B_+>1/b>1, so all cap hypotheses hold. For a chronological prefix
with mass U of this ACTUAL H_j, the point is exactly

    x=-A_j+U.

Indeed 0<=U<=S_j=A_j-1 implies x<=-1, so every queried derivative is
-1 and the GD update advances U by the next actual step. This is an
induction on physical prefixes with all region guards shown, not an
evaluated trajectory. The true terminal is x_n=-1 and p_n=1.

Its original radius is R=A_j=d_j*Bmax, whereas (3.2) gives

    a*Bmax=a*A_j/d_j=A_j/c^j<1=p_n.

Thus (7.1) fails at its q=0 boundary. Both fixed caps are retained and
the complete terminal is on its own cap knot. Moreover

    a<K/Bmax=d_j/A_j<b,

so this is precisely a permitted unbalanced cap pair, even arbitrarily
near the balance threshold as the parameters vary.

It is NOT a counterexample to (G): the noncrossing proof already gives
p_n< b*R/d_j<beta*R/d_j. This test shows why merely copying the old
balanced grid cannot decide mechanism1. It supplies no authorization
to invoke mechanism2's weaker-global-bound alternative, which requires
an actual counterexample to (G) itself.

## 8. Why unrestricted crossing-gain products cannot close the proof

For a general list of t crossings with physical levels k_1,...,k_t and
noncrossing gap masses M_0,...,M_t, repeated use of (2.2)-(2.3) gives

    p_n/R <= product_i d_(k_i) / product_r (1+M_r). (8.1)

This is an upper relaxation. If its right side is too large, nothing
has been proved about the existence of a corresponding actual history.
Sections4-5 used it only where the full physical mass bound sufficed.

Here the relaxation itself can exceed the desired coefficient. Fix
L=8 and j=m, and formally select every bridge of level at least L as a
crossing. Each gap is a literal H_L, with mass S_L. The coarse word has
2^(m-L)-1 selected bridges and 2^(m-L) such gaps. Put t=m-L and

    q_L=c*d_L/A_L.

Counting each coarse level's physical multiplicity symbolically gives

    product(selected d)/A_L^(2^t)
       =q_L^(2^t)/(c*d_m).                         (8.2)

This is an exact formula for the RELAXATION, not an executed word or
a legal itinerary. Its derivation uses
sum_(r=0)^(t-1) 2^(t-1-r)=2^t-1 and
sum_(r=0)^(t-1) r*2^(t-1-r)=2^t-t-1.

For completeness it is analytically larger than beta/d_m throughout
the assigned m range. Expanding cb gives

    1+6epsilon<cb<1+8epsilon,
    b/a-1=(cb-beta)/beta<10epsilon.

By (3.1),

    q_L=cb/[1+(b/a-1)*(2/c)^L].

Since 2/c<5/6 and (5/6)^8<1/4 (equivalently
4*390625<1679616), at L=8 this gives

    q_8>(1+6epsilon)/(1+(5/2)epsilon)>1+3epsilon.

The last inequality is equivalent to
(1/2)epsilon-(15/2)epsilon^2>0, valid here. At m=16 one has
2^(m-8)>4m, and doubling preserves that inequality for every subsequent
integer m. Thus it certainly holds at m>=1536. The finite binomial
inequality yields

    q_8^(2^(m-8))>1+3epsilon*2^(m-8)>4>c*beta.

Inserting this in (8.2) proves failure of the proposed universal
combinatorial upper bound on the relaxed product. No giant integer,
trajectory, recurrence, or numerical table was computed.

The equality pattern of this relaxation is incompatible with the SAME
fixed derivative. In particular, equality in a gap H_8 would require
all its queried gradients to have a common magnitude p>0, its last
source radius to equal p, and its initial radius to equal A_8*p.
The condition G_sigma(p)=p forces kappa=1: for kappa<1 both kappa*p
and p-(1-kappa) are strictly below p. With kappa=1, constancy over the
nonempty radius interval from p to A_8*p forces the fixed ray cap to
be exactly p. Thus equality at consecutive visits to the same ray
forces the same p.

At the first two formal coarse crossings the levels are8 and9. Equality
would give source magnitudes

    p_2=(d_8/A_8)*p_1,
    p_3=(d_9/A_8)*p_2=c*(d_8/A_8)^2*p_1.

The third source is back on the first ray, so the fixed cap would
require p_3=p_1. But

    c*(d_8/A_8)^2<c*b^2=gamma<1/2.

This is a contradiction. It does not prove that the whole formal
crossing pattern is impossible with strict losses, nor that all actual
patterns satisfy (G). It shows exactly why the relaxed growing product
is not an actual counterexample and why fixed-ray consistency cannot
be dropped from the unresolved proof.

## 9. What remains genuinely open after these derivations

Mechanism1 is undecided. The direct partial results reduce its remaining
positive-terminal histories to all the following simultaneous conditions:

* at least THREE strict crossings in the actual complete H_j;
* terminal strictly before its own cap knot;
* if the terminal is on the larger ray,

      0<R<b*d_j*K/beta and 0<p_n<b*K;

  if it is on the smaller ray,

      0<R<gamma*d_j*K/beta and 0<p_n<gamma*K;

* one fixed derivative throughout every return to a ray, with the
  original signed start s and enclosing m unchanged.

These conditions describe the remaining class for the NEW direct
argument. The earlier balanced report supplies a separate JOINT
UNVERIFIED candidate for cap ratio K/B>=b. Keeping that candidate at
its stated status, the unresolved interface of the overall two-cap
programme is the above class with K/B<b. No accepted theorem removes
that class, and its mere description is not a new invariant or a proof.

The small original radius here need not lie inside the common core or
below the smaller cap knot: d_j can be large. Neither p_n<gamma*K nor
p_n<b*K produces the necessary original-radius decay in that class.
The two direct routes have concrete failures: the unrestricted a-grid
is legally refuted by Section7, and the free alternating-run product
is refuted AS A RELAXATION by Section8. Neither failure is a legal
counterexample to (G). A proof must retain and exploit the quantitative
loss from using the same two caps and common inner derivative over
three or more crossings.

Because the required decisive first result was not obtained,
mechanism2's all-cap Huber reference, objective assembly and weaker-
global-bound alternative were NOT initiated. The already paid smaller-
tail terminals do not by themselves close the remaining pre-cap class.
No claim of the complete scalar theorem or the full W hypothesis is
made. The original minimax problem remains OPEN, W_n=2*mathcal R_n.

## 10. Checks, provenance, and resource closure

The fixed family is never replaced during a history. All signs, cap
knots, kappa=1, collapsed middle pieces, zero hits, level0 empty children,
actual crossing positions and exterior ancestor masses are accounted
for in the proofs above. The strict mass inequalities use the actual
same-m condition a<b, not the false identity cb=1.

The original-radius estimates in Sections4-5 use full original prefixes
and suffixes. Section6 is honestly a cap-scale estimate except where
the explicit original-radius threshold is imposed. Section7 is an
actual complete H_j example, but refutes only its labeled stronger
grid. Section8 is explicitly an unrealized relaxation. Its fixed-cap
incompatibility argument concerns equality, not every nearby history.

This is a textual self-screen, not independent or formal verification.

# Complete normal spectral mixtures remain insufficient after free completion

Status: complete candidate proof, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit is allocated.
Accepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and maximal completion),
dfde2fa326c908bf (complex orbit notation and necessary interpolation), and
b98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark recursions). The recursive family and
the real-product method were recalled from PUBLIC candidate79f6ce08b41616b5;
no unverified result is used below. Every needed estimate is included.

The final result is stronger than the supporting individual-mode statement
in Sections1--5: Sections6--7 prove a common unit-step O(c^(-m)) upper bound
for EVERY jointly legal finite normal spectral mixture, with arbitrary complex
spectra, arbitrary horizon-dependent weights and dimension, arbitrary legal
prefix values, and a fully free terminal completion. NO individual-mode
legality is assumed in that final result. A single common interpolation
cycle controls all troublesome modes simultaneously. The exact benchmark
comparison is included, so this is a full obstruction for the stated normal
polynomial-prefix ansatz, not just for separately realizable modes.

## Supporting individual-mode result

Put c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and define

    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    N_m=2^m-1,
    S_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

For a complex z, let x_i=P_i(z) be the chronological prefix polynomial
orbit of H_m, starting from x_0=1, and g_i=z x_i for i=0,...,N_m. Include
the optimum jet (0,0,0). Its complete-block polynomial is

    p_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-b_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)).

Suppose this FULL prefix is interpolable by a convex differentiable
1-smooth function with minimum zero at the origin. Its values may be ANY
legal interpolating values, not only the maximal path values of the
complex-orbit construction. At the appended final point, use ANY convex
1-smooth interpolant F of the chosen jets, including the pointwise maximal
one and with no condition on its terminal gradient. Then the COMMON unit
step satisfies

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m)) <= 2c^11/(a c^m).                      (1)

This holds for every m>=1, with deliberately nonoptimal constants.
It also holds for any finite orthogonal mixture of such INDIVIDUALLY legal
complex orbits with squared initial weights summing to one, even if the
joint legal function values and final interpolant are not separable.

The canonical sum-only Huber channel on the appended schedule (H_m,1)
is also O(c^(-m)). The exact recursions imply

    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3),

so, since c^3>16, the maximum of the entire stated witness class and that
Huber channel, divided by U_(2^m)^ZJ, tends to zero.

This supporting result is a scoped upper bound on the stated witness family,
NOT on actual W_(2^m)(H_m,1). It uses full schedule-dependent legality, with
no universal spectral lens. By itself it does not cover jointly legal
mixtures of individually illegal modes. Sections6--7 remove that restriction
using one common cycle. General nonnormal kernels remain outside the scope
even after that extension.

## 1. Universal last-query inequalities and the free unit step

Write z=alpha+i beta and sigma=|z|^2. For z=0 all gradients vanish and
convexity makes every queried value, and the appended unchanged value,
zero. Hence assume z!=0. Interpolation between x_0=1 and the optimum gives

    sigma<=alpha,  alpha>0,  |z|<=1,
    f_0<=alpha-sigma/2.                                      (2)

For any queried i it likewise gives

    f_i<= (alpha-sigma/2)|x_i|^2.                             (3)

The unit-step descent inequality, obtained by integrating a 1-Lipschitz
gradient along -g_i, gives F(x_i-g_i)<=f_i-|g_i|^2/2. Thus for the last
query, independently of all earlier value choices,

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))
       <=2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2.                           (4)

This includes the maximal terminal completion in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.

## 2. Full interpolation imposes a sector or a small-parameter case

Put q=alpha/|z| in(0,1] and h_c=2alpha/sigma. Then h_c>=2 by(2), and
|1-hz|<=1 exactly for0<=h<=h_c.

If every step in H_m is smaller than h_c, every factor contracts, so
|p_m(z)|<=1. Also b_(m-1)<h_c implies

    |z|^2 < 2alpha/b_(m-1) <=2|z|/b_(m-1),
    |z|<2c/(a c^m).

Equation(4) is then less than4c/(a c^m), which is at most the bound(1).
The same conclusion follows if there is no step greater than or equal to
h_c. We now treat the remaining case.

Let r be the smallest level with b_r>=h_c. Then r<=m-1, b_(r-1)<h_c,
and r>=18 because b_17=1<h_c. The chronological prefix before the first
b_r is exactly H_r, and every step of this prefix strictly contracts radii.
Define v=b_r|z|. Minimality gives

    2q<=v<2c q.                                              (5)

Let d=2/c=10/13. Since d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5, d^17<1/40. Consequently

    S_r/b_r=(1-d^r)/(c-2)>=13/8.                             (6)

The squared-step sum of H_r is

    T_r=sum_(h in H_r) h^2=a^2(c^(2r)-2^r)/(c^2-2),
    T_r/b_r^2<=1/(c^2-2)=25/119.                             (7)

Using log(1+t)<=t in every squared radial factor gives

    log|p_r(z)|^2 <= -2alpha S_r+sigma T_r
                   <=-(13/4)q v+v^2/(c^2-2).                 (8)

The concave quadratic (13/4)q v-v^2/(c^2-2) has its minimum on the
interval(5) at an endpoint. At v=2q its coefficient of q^2 is
13/2-100/119=1347/238>11/2. At v=2c q that coefficient is
169/10-676/119>11/2. Thus

    |p_r(z)|^2 <= exp(-(11/2)q^2).                            (9)

We now use ACTUAL ordered value inequalities inside the prefix, not only
pairwise cocoercivity or radial contraction. At a step of size h with point
x and next point x_+=(1-hz)x, interpolation in the direction from the next
query back to this query gives

    f(x)-f(x_+)
      >=h(1-halpha+hsigma/2)sigma |x|^2
      =[sigma/(2alpha)](|x|^2-|x_+|^2)
        +[h^2 sigma/(2alpha)]
              [sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2]|x|^2.                 (10)

For the first inequality, g_+=(1-hz)g,
<g_+,g>=(1-halpha)|g|^2, and |g-g_+|^2=h^2sigma|g|^2. Expanding gives
the displayed identity; no endpoint value or local oracle is guessed.

Suppose q<=2/3. Then sigma-2alpha^2=sigma(1-2q^2)>0, so the second term
in(10) is nonnegative. Sum(10) along the exact H_r prefix. Its terminal
value is nonnegative and (2) bounds the initial value, so

    [sigma/(2alpha)](1-|p_r(z)|^2)<=alpha-sigma/2,
    |p_r(z)|^2>=1-2q^2+alpha>1-2q^2.                         (11)

But for0<t<=4/9,

    exp(-(11/2)t)<1-2t.                                     (12)

Indeed the difference 1-2t-exp(-(11/2)t) is concave, vanishes at0, and is
positive at4/9. For the last claim,
exp(22/9)=exp(2)exp(4/9)>(8/3)^2(1+4/9)=832/81>9;
exp(1)>8/3 follows from the first four terms of its positive series.
Equations(9),(11),(12) contradict each other. Therefore every legal orbit
in the present case satisfies

    q=Re(z)/|z|>2/3.                                        (13)

This is a necessary condition derived from the entire contracting prefix.

## 3. Uniform damping of the dyadic product in the forced sector

Assume0<|z|<=1 and q=Re(z)/|z|>=2/3. Choose the unique integer k>=17
such that

    c^k a|z|<=1<c^(k+1)a|z|,
    y=c^k a|z| in(1/c,1].                                  (14)

If m<=k, every factor has b_j|z|<=1, and
|1-b_jz|^2=1-2q b_j|z|+b_j^2|z|^2<=1; hence |p_m(z)|<=1.

Suppose m>k. A vanishing factor makes the desired damping immediate, so
assume there is none. Write z/|z|=exp(i theta). Directly from multiplicities,

    log|p_m(z)|/2^(m-k-1)
       =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1) 2^(-j)log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|.    (15)

For j<0, log(1+t)<=t gives

    log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=-q c^j y+(c^j y)^2/2.

Summing these terms and using k>=17 and (6) bounds their contribution by

    -(13/4)q y + (25/119)y^2.

For j>=1, c^j y>1 and q>=2/3, so
|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=c^j y. The bounds jlog c+log y are nonnegative;
extending their weighted sum to all j>=1 gives2log c+log y. The j=0 term
is at most(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2). Therefore(15) is at most

    E(y)=-(13/6)y+(25/119)y^2
           +(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2)+log y+2log(13/5).          (16)

Here we used q>=2/3 also in the negative linear term. We prove the fully
uniform elementary inequality

    E(y)<-1/128  for5/13<=y<=1.                              (17)

First let5/13<=y<=1/2. The positive quadratic term is at most25/476,
and D(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2 is decreasing here, so D(y)<=322/507. Also
-(13/6)y+log y<=-1+log(6/13), by maximizing that elementary concave
function. Combining yields

    E(y)<=-1+25/476+log A,
    A=(78/25)sqrt(322/507),  A^2=3864/625<(5/2)^2.

We have log(5/2)<15/16, since the first four terms in the positive
exponential series give

    exp(15/16)>
      1+15/16+(15/16)^2/2+(15/16)^3/6
      =61791/24576>5/2.

Thus E(y)<-1+25/476+15/16=-19/1904<-1/128.

For1/2<=y<=1, E is strictly decreasing. Here are the exact derivative
calculations to check that claim, without a numerical maximization. With
D(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2,

    E'(y)=B(y)/[2142yD(y)],
    B(y)=900y^4-5841y^3+11372y^2-8925y+2142.

Put t=2y-1 in[0,1]. Direct expansion gives

    16B((1+t)/2)
       =-2422-11870t+15842t^2-8082t^3+900t^4
       <=-2422+t[-11870+15842t-7182t^2]
       <=-2422-3210t<0.

For the second inequality, 15842t-7182t^2 is increasing on[0,1] and is
at most8660. The denominator of E' is positive. Hence E(y)<=E(1/2),
already controlled by the preceding interval. This proves(17).

Equations(15)--(17) imply

    |p_m(z)| <= exp(-2^(m-k-1)/128)  whenever m>k.             (18)

## 4. The free terminal objective bound

For a legal orbit the small-parameter case in Section2 already gives
the bound(1). Otherwise(13) permits the product estimates of Section3.
If |z|<=1/(a c^m), then m<=k and(4) is at most2/(a c^m).
If |z|>1/(a c^m), let r=m-k>=1. By(14),(18),

    2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2
       <=[2/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^(r-1)/64)
       <=2c^11/(a c^m).                                    (19)

For1<=r<=11, discard the exponential and use c^r<=c^11. For r>=12,
2^(r-1)/64=2^(r-7)>=r, by induction from r=12, while log c<1 because
exp(1)>8/3>13/5. The extra factor is then at most1. This proves(19),
and therefore(1), with complete prefix values and arbitrary terminal
completion retained.

For a finite orthogonal mixture, write its components as

    x_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l),
    g_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l),
    w_l>=0, sum_l w_l=1,

up to harmless rotations in the individual planes. Assume every active
z_l individually has a legal prefix. For ANY jointly legal values and ANY
joint smooth convex interpolant, the same last-query/unit-step inequalities
give

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))
       <=2sum_l w_l(Re(z_l)-|z_l|^2)|p_m(z_l)|^2
       <=2c^11/(a c^m).

Separability of the joint interpolant is unnecessary. Individual legality
IS needed to apply Section2 to each z_l; it is not inferred from joint
legality. No such inference is claimed for mixtures of illegal modes.

## 5. Exact-profile comparison, with its recurrence estimate proved here

For m>=17, (6) with r=m gives S_m>=13a c^m/8. Thus the canonical Huber
value for(H_m,1) is at most

    1/[1+2(S_m+1)]<=1/(2S_m)<=4/(13a c^m).                    (20)

We now derive B_n<=1+4n^(4/3) directly from the exact recursions stated in
acceptedb98bb807d514e80d. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.
In these variables the primitive and dominant joins respectively are

    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

They are increasing in their nonnegative arguments. For0<=t<=1,

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.                  (21)

To prove it set x=t^(1/3),y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. Then

    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4
       =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.

Each reciprocal is at least1/3, while xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3; the latter strict
inequality follows by cubing. The endpoints also satisfy the identity.
Since Omega is the positive root of X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, (21) implies

    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).

Induction in the primitive recursion gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also
sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence G(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the dominant
recursion, for every split k, gives

    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3).

The initial values are C_0=B_0=1. The last inequality uses the elementary
superadditivity of u^(4/3) on nonnegative u.

The appended horizon is N_m+1=2^m. Multiply(1),(20) by
B_(2^m)<=1+4*2^(4m/3). Their ratios to U_(2^m)^ZJ tend to zero because
c^3=2197/125>16. This closes the proposed all-schedule comparison for the
entire stated individually legal orbit/direct-sum family, even after free
terminal completion and addition of the canonical Huber channel.

## 6. Analytic estimates for supporting modes, independent of legality

The estimates in this section assume only0<|z|<=1 and
q=Re(z)/|z|>=2/3; they do not assume that the orbit is individually legal.
Retain k from(14), and define

    R_u(z)=p_u(z)(1-b_u z),
    E_u(z)=sum_(i in H_u) h_i^2 |z|^2 |P_i(z)|^2.

Here P_i are the local chronological prefix polynomials of H_u starting
at1. The recursion of the actual ordered list gives

    E_(u+1)=(1+|R_u|^2)E_u+b_u^2|z|^2|p_u|^2.               (22)

For u<=k all factors contract, so E_k<=|z|^2 T_k<1. At u=k both
|R_k|^2 and b_k^2|z|^2|p_k|^2 are at most1, hence E_(k+1)<=3.
For u=k+t,t>=1, the damping estimate(18) and b_u|z|<=c^t give

    |R_u|^2, b_u^2|z|^2|p_u|^2
          <=c^(2t)exp(-2^(t-7)).                            (23)

For1<=t<=12 this is at most c^24. For t>=13,2^(t-7)>=3t, by induction
from t=13, so(23) is at most exp(-t)<=2^(-t), using log c<1. Applying(22)
to E_u+1 therefore gives the uniform bound

    E_u(z)+1<K_E:=12(1+c^24)^12  for every u>=0.               (24)

Indeed E_(k+1)+1<=4. The next twelve updates multiply this upper bound
by at most(1+c^24)^12. Every subsequent update multiplies E_u+1 by at
most1+2^(-t); their product is at most exp(sum_(t>=13)2^(-t))<exp(1)<3.
For exp(1)<3, sum n!>=2^(n-1), with strictness when n>=3, in the
exponential series. The earlier u<=k already satisfy(24).

There is also the weighted block-amplitude bound

    |z||R_u(z)|^2<=c^36/b_u.                                (25)

If u<=k, |z|<=1/b_u and |R_u|<=1. If u=k+t>k, multiply(23) by
|z|<=c^t/b_u. For t<=12 the result is at most c^36/b_u; for t>=13 it
is at most1/b_u because2^(t-7)>=3t and log c<1. In particular

    Re(z)|R_(m-1)(z)|^2<=c^37/b_m.                          (26)

These are quantitative bounds on the same chronological orbit, with no
dimension, mixture-weight or function-value assumptions.

## 7. One common complete cycle controls ALL normal modes

Consider ANY finite list of complex numbers z_l and weights w_l>=0 with
sum_l w_l=1. Form the real direct-sum data for the entire H_m prefix by

    x_i=(sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l))_l,
    g_i=(sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l))_l,
    x_star=g_star=0.                                        (27)

Real scalar modes are included as beta_l=0. Constant phases in individual
planes can be absorbed by orthogonal rotations. There is no assumption on
the individual spectra or their separate legality. Assume only that these
JOINT data admit actual convex1-smooth interpolation values f_i, with the
given optimum, and let F be ANY legal interpolant of them.

Define constants depending only on c by

    C_aux=K_E c^37+2c[1+4c^2/(c^2-2)],
    C_norm=2c^11+256 C_aux.                                  (28)

We prove the uniform upper bound

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))<=C_norm/(a c^m).                     (29)

The spectra, weights, number of modes, and legal values may all depend on
m. This upper bound includes the pointwise maximal terminal completion.

### 7a. The common cycle and its exact recurrence

For any mode z=alpha+i beta, put sigma=|z|^2, kappa=alpha-sigma, and let

    L_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r) h_i |P_i(z)|^2,
    E_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r) h_i^2 sigma |P_i(z)|^2.

Define its reverse-chronological optimum cycle weight by

    D_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r)
                 h_i sigma(1-h_i alpha+h_i sigma/2)|P_i(z)|^2
                  +(sigma/2)|p_r(z)|^2-alpha+sigma/2.        (30)

The cycle goes from the optimum to the end of H_r, backwards through its
queries to its start, then to the optimum. The reverse query edges have
the algebraic weights in(10). Its area and energy alternatively give

    D_r(z)=beta^2 L_r(z)
                 -(kappa/2)[1+|p_r(z)|^2+E_r(z)].            (31)

To check(31), each forward GD edge has area
Im(conj(P_i)P_(i+1))=-h_i beta|P_i|^2. Reversing the path changes the
sign, and the two optimum edges contribute zero area. The cycle energy is
1+|p_r|^2+E_r. The complex cycle identity of accepteddfde2fa326c908bf is
purely algebraic and therefore applies even to an individually illegal mode.

If alpha>0, telescoping squared norms yields

    1-|p_r|^2=2alpha L_r-E_r.

Put d= sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2. Substitution into(31) gives

    D_r=[d/(2alpha)](1+E_r)
                    -[sigma(1-alpha)/(2alpha)]|p_r|^2.

Using(22), p_(r+1)=R_r p_r, and
b_r^2sigma|p_r|^2-|R_r|^2=(2b_r alpha-1)|p_r|^2, one obtains the EXACT
recurrence

    D_(r+1)=(1+|R_r|^2)D_r
                         +|p_r|^2[kappa+b_r d].             (32)

For clarity, in the substitution the constant-coefficient difference is
[sigma(1-alpha)-d]/(2alpha)=kappa. Thus no sign in(32) is inferred from
legality or a guessed potential.

Now use the LAST copy of H_(m-1) inside H_m. Its start polynomial is
R_(m-1), and its end polynomial is p_m=R_(m-1)p_(m-1). The SAME indexed
cycle on the full joint prefix has weight

    sum_l w_l D_(m-1)(z_l)|R_(m-1)(z_l)|^2.

Joint interpolation requires

    sum_l w_l D_(m-1)(z_l)|R_(m-1)(z_l)|^2<=0.               (33)

No different cycle is selected for different spectral modes in(33).

### 7b. Classification of every complex parameter

Partition the active modes into the following three groups.

OUTSIDE MODES: kappa<=0. Formula(31) gives D_r>=0 for every r, because
beta^2 L_r>=0 and the bracketed energy is nonnegative. These modes can
supply NO negative credit in(33), and their unit-step objective contribution
2w_l kappa_l|p_m(z_l)|^2 is nonpositive. This includes arbitrary real or
complex parameters outside the cocoercivity disk and the zero mode.

SAFE MODES: kappa>0 and either q=alpha/|z|>=2/3, or every step of H_m is
smaller than h_c=2alpha/sigma. Here alpha>=sigma>0 implies0<|z|<=1.
In the sector case, Sections3 and4 are purely polynomial bounds and give

    2kappa|p_m|^2<=2c^11/b_m.                               (34)

Also each term in(30) is at least-alpha h_i^2sigma|P_i|^2, so
-D_r<=alpha(1+E_r). With(24),(26), this yields

    [-D_(m-1)]|R_(m-1)|^2<=K_E c^37/b_m.                   (35)

In the all-contracting case, every prefix modulus is at most1 and
|z|<2c/b_m. Then E_(m-1)<=|z|^2 T_(m-1)<=4c^2/(c^2-2), and the left
side of(35) is at most2c[1+4c^2/(c^2-2)]/b_m. The objective bound(34)
also holds, by the small-parameter calculation of Section2. Thus the total
negative cycle credit from ALL safe modes, after their weights are applied,
is at most C_aux/b_m, while their total objective contribution is at most
2c^11/b_m.

TROUBLESOME MODES: kappa>0, q<2/3, and there is a level r<=m-1 with
b_r>=h_c. Take the first such r for the following ANALYSIS of the mode,
without changing the common cycle(33). It has r>=18, and all of H_r
strictly contracts. Estimates(5)--(9) and the algebraic identity in(10)
do not need individual legality. They imply

    D_r(z)>=alpha[(1-exp(-(11/2)q^2))/(2q^2)-1].              (36)

The bracket decreases as q^2>0 increases: differentiating
(1-exp(-at))/t and using exp(at)>=1+at proves this. Since q^2<=4/9,

    D_r(z)/alpha
       >=(9/8)(1-exp(-22/9))-1
       >(9/8)(1-81/832)-1
       =103/6656>1/128.                                    (37)

Here we used the explicit exponential bound in(12). Moreover
d=sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2>0 and kappa>0, so(32) implies for every u>=r

    D_(u+1)>=|R_u|^2 D_u.

Propagating the positive defect up to m-1 gives

    D_(m-1)(z)
       >=D_r(z) product_(u=r)^(m-2)|R_u(z)|^2
       >=[alpha/128]|p_(m-1)(z)|^2.                        (38)

For the second inequality, p_(m-1)=p_r product_(u=r)^(m-2)R_u and
|p_r|<=1. The empty product when r=m-1 is allowed. Multiplying(38) by
|R_(m-1)|^2 proves that EVERY troublesome mode contributes at least

    [alpha/128]|p_m(z)|^2                                 (39)

to the SAME cycle(33). Thus troublesome modes cannot cancel each other's
positive defects on this cycle, even if their first violating scales differ.

These three groups cover every complex number. Boundary kappa=0 modes
were placed outside, and q=2/3 modes in the safe sector, so no limiting case
is omitted.

### 7c. Joint legality pays for the whole problematic spectral mass

Insert the classification into(33). Outside modes contribute nonnegative
cycle weights; all troublesome modes have the simultaneous lower bound(39).
The total possible negative credit of safe modes is at most C_aux/b_m.
Therefore

    sum_(troublesome l) w_l alpha_l |p_m(z_l)|^2
                                      <=128 C_aux/b_m.       (40)

For ANY joint legal values, interpolation to the optimum followed by the
common unit-step descent gives

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))
      <=2<x_(N_m),g_(N_m)>-2|g_(N_m)|^2
      =2sum_l w_l kappa_l |p_m(z_l)|^2.                      (41)

Outside modes have nonpositive terms. Safe modes contribute at most
2c^11/b_m in total. In every troublesome mode kappa_l<=alpha_l, so(40)
bounds their total by256 C_aux/b_m. This proves(29).

No assumption of separable function values, separate legality, a bounded
number of modes, or a spectrum fixed across horizons was made. The Gram
data in(27) are real and PSD by actual direct-sum realization; joint
legality, when it holds, is the only hypothesis used for(33),(41).

## 8. Exact scope of the completed normal-mixture obstruction

Every real finite-dimensional normal operator is orthogonally decomposable
into real scalar blocks and planar complex-multiplication blocks. For a
normalized initial vector, the squared block-coordinate norms give exactly
the weights in(27); initial phases are harmless planar rotations. Therefore
(29) applies equivalently to EVERY jointly legal prefix of the form

    x_i=P_i(A)x_0,  g_i=A x_i,

with one real normal operator A, arbitrary real Gram dimension, arbitrary
legal values, and arbitrary free terminal completion. No spectral restriction
on A is imposed. The finite direct-sum formulation(27) also states the
whole result without using operator terminology.

The horizon after the unit step is2^m. Multiplying(29) and the canonical
Huber bound(20) by B_(2^m)<=1+4*2^(4m/3), proved in Section5, makes both
ratios tend to zero, since c^3>16. This is a full rigorous candidate
obstruction for the chosen NORMAL spectral-mixture ansatz. It does not
upper-bound unrestricted W or exclude a genuinely NONNORMAL kernel.

## Remaining possible repairs

1. Jointly repairing illegal normal modes works in the finite3D calibration,
   but Sections6--7 show it cannot restore the benchmark uniformly on H_m.
   More such normal modes or altered legal values do not avoid(29).
2. A definite nonnormal polynomial kernel can have correlations absent from
   every normal spectral mixture. Replacing it by an arbitrary Gram form
   would merely restate the original problem and is not a quantitative repair.
3. The legal growing Chebyshev constructions remain valid scoped candidates.
   They do not supply the missing uniform comparison on this recursive family.

Multiplying every interpolant by an arbitrary prescribed L>0 restores the
project's smoothness normalization without changing these normalized bounds.
